Vienna → Baron Schenck at Cracow · alphabetical homophonic table with a code · March–July 1706

A Vienna agent to Baron Schenck, 1706

Fifteen German letters from Vienna to a chamberlain of Augustus II, catalogued as “from Dresden”: their letter cipher was recovered from the ciphertext alone, but most of their code numbers for people stay open.

Daniel Bourdeau · posted 15 October 2026 · updated 23 October 2026

Method: key recovered from ciphertext-only · Extent: partial (46% of the cipher)

Credits · Contemporary key or decipherment (key or decipherment used)

ciphertext-only
On this pageThe recordsThe Vienna letters to SchenckThe letter tableThe deciphermentThe codesMethodWhat remains uncertainFull textsSources

At a glanceCiphertext-only break · partial (46% of the cipher)

The letters
Fifteen letters, 7 March to 14 July 1706, from an agent in Vienna who signs with a cover name read “Sidon”, to the Baron de Schenck, chamberlain of King Augustus II, at Cracow and Lublin.
Where
Archiwum Narodowe w Krakowie, AKM 21 plik 30/1 (DECODE R7486–R7500), catalogued on DECODE as from “NN z Drezna (jezuita?)”, with no decipherment.
The cipher
Clear German with single words and phrases in a homophonic letter table (a = 7, 8, 9; b = 10, 11, 12; … three numbers to a letter in alphabetical order), three-digit code numbers for people and things, and dotted letter signs.
How it was deciphered
A free solver gave nothing; a search that kept the table in alphabetical blocks and moved only the block boundaries found it from the ciphertext alone. The recipient’s own glosses agree with it and give three words of the code: der, von, der Kayser. Words of the code’s alphabetical 500s and a few persons were then fixed from context.
What it says
Court news from Vienna in the year of Ramillies and Turin, the Saxon king's affairs at the imperial court, rivalries for a post, the Malcontents in Hungary: “dieser glaubet sey kein guttes omen”.
Still open
Ten letters (R7486, R7488, R7489, R7490, R7492, R7493, R7496, R7497, R7498, R7500) break in most lines on open person codes, dotted signs and, in R7493, water damage: decoded, not read. R7487, R7491, R7494, R7495 and R7499 build sentences (22–23 October 2026) but read at 90.1%, 92.5%, 86.7%, 92.4% and 91.7% of their words (measured 24 October 2026), under the 95% a complete reading needs. 65 of the 77 three-digit codes, nearly all persons (187, 181, 166, 190 …): their roles are clear, their names are not, and no key survives. Also eight short runs and about ninety small word signs.

On DECODE these fifteen letters were catalogued with eighteen letters to Hetman Adam Mikołaj Sieniawski under one range of records (R7468–R7500, catalogue entry 333). Those are other correspondents in four other keys, each with its own page (a different cipher in each): Brâncoveanu and Cantacuzino, news from Bender, 1709, an adviser, c. 1707 and Chomentowski at the Porte.

01 The records

DECODE R7486–R7500, Archiwum Narodowe w Krakowie, AKM 21 plik 30/1, items 1–15, uploaded in September 2023 with status N/A, cipher type “unknown” and no key or decryption file. DECODE gives the sender as “NN z Drezna (jezuita?)” and the receiver as “Schenck (Szench) szambelan Augusta II”. The neighbouring records R7460–R7467 and R7501–R7507 belong to other collections and correspondents and give no key for these.

02 The Vienna letters to Schenck

The address leaf of the letter of 27 March reads A Monsieur / Mons.r Le Baron de Schenck / Chambell. de Sa Majesté / Le Roy de Pologne / à Cracovie. Every letter is dated from Vienna (“W. d. 12. Maj 1706”), not Dresden; the writer numbers them (N. 15 on 27 March, N. 47 on 14 July, so more than one a week), closes “Votre tres humble et tres obeiss. Serviteur” and signs with a name read “Sidon” or “Sadon”, probably a cover name. The receipt dockets say “Recept. d. 18 May 1706 in Cracau”, “d. 7. Junÿ 1706 in Lublin”; a later archive hand wrote “Seligman” and “Lehman” on two address leaves.

DECODENo.DateDECODENo.Date
R7486187 March 1706R7494355 June
R7487–13 MarchR74953815 June
R74881527 MarchR74963916 June
R7489278 MayR74974019 June
R74902812 MayR74984123 June
R74913122 MayR7499443 July
R7492–26 MayR75004714 July
R74933229 May

Most of each letter is in clear German, mixed with French and Latin: the siege of Landau and the fall of Hagenau, Barcelona relieved, Ramillies and the coming peace congress (who might mediate, Denmark or Sweden), the relief of Turin, the Malcontents in Hungary. The cipher covers names and the delicate words.

03 The letter table

Three numbers to each letter, in alphabetical order, with no j and no v:

abcdefghiklm
7 8 910 11 1213 14 1516 17 1819 20 2122 23 2425 26 2728 29 3031 32 3334 35 3637 38 3940 41 42
nopqrstuw, vyz
43 44 4546 47 4849 50 5152 53 5455 56 5758 59 6061 62 6364 65 6667 68 69 7076 77 7879

The end of the table is irregular: 70 is w (wieder, wegen), 76–78 are y (Ogilvy, Convoy, Secretarÿ), 79 is z (zugleich, correspondenz, Reichsvicecantzler); 67–69 serve for both v and w (von, Vanitäten, Avisen). The single digits 7, 8 and 9 inside runs are therefore a. No key sheet survives with the letters.

The recipient worked some runs out himself. Over “58 31 15 28 25 19 58 14 28 37 8 26 19 43” (sich geschlagen) in N. 18 he wrote small letters s, a, k, l, a, and over “… 51 32 55 19 62” (dessipiret) p, r, e, t: they agree with the table.

04 The decipherment

106 runs of the letter table, 682 numbers. Quoted from the readings of 22–23 October 2026 (reading/<R>_reading.md, English _en.md): the clear German re-read on the images, the runs printed as the words they spell, codes and dotted signs that have a value in {braces}, open person codes as [numbers], open dotted signs in <angles>.

  • “Dieser glaubet, [es] sey kein guttes omen alhier”; “{Der} Herr Senfft hat gar <3> [181] abgesezt, {und} <e> [190] sich geschlagen”; “{Der} [190] [?soll] hier viel Cabalen wieder [181] vorgehabt haben”; “{Der} Reichsvicecantzler” (N. 18, 7 March; decoded, not read).
  • “von {König Augustus} Ihm eine convoy […]” (N. 15, decoded, not read).
  • “de Baron Kleinburg alhier habe”, “a[u]s [?Muscau] geschrieben” (N. 28, 12 May; decoded, not read).
  • “wie auch beygleich {von dem} {König Augustus} ein rescri[…]pt, so {von dem} [154] {und} [166] zugleich unterschrieben”; “ob vielleicht eine combination {der} [517] {von dem} [154] {und} [166] geschehen”; “{Ordre} du cabinet”; “in puncto diversitatis religionis” (N. 38, 15 June; read).
  • “wegen introduc[?t]ion [237] <c> [220] {und} [207] admission”, “insonderheit wegen [?Nellenbug]”, “von wem die semina der diffidence ausgestreuet worden” (N. 41, 23 June; decoded, not read).
  • “<oo> malcontenten auffs neue [?allarmiren] dürfften”, “schon [?accordirten] [?puncten] wieder [?resiliren] wolle”, “[?dimission]” (N. 47, 14 July; decoded, not read).

638 of the 682 numbers (93.5%) read as sense. Seven runs that a single slip spoils still read (introduc[t]ion, punc[t]en, d[i]mission, be[m]ühen); eight do not: “gutswor”, “sollwiglaufeenden” (perhaps zuwiderlauffenden, with the recipient's a u f over it), “lceth”, “ditlan”, “datmseyihm” and three of one or two letters (“iwzo” is ijzo, the writer’s spelling of itzo). Their digits were checked twice on the images.

Which letters read. A number given a value is not a reading, and the reading audit of 22 October 2026 judged each letter as text. On the same day every doubtful word of the letters then unread was re-read on the 32 DECODE images (about 170 corrections, mostly a hard hand rather than lost ink), and readings with English were written for every letter. Five letters build German sentences with the person codes left as numbers: R7487, R7491, R7494, R7495 and R7499. In the other ten the sentences break on the open person codes and dotted signs (R7486, R7488, R7489, R7490, R7492, R7496, and on 23 October R7497, R7498 and R7500, which fall just under the bar once their running readings are written out), and in R7493 on water damage to the lower halves of its pages. Those ten are decoded, not read; their partial readings stay in the repository. The share of the cipher in letters that read is about a quarter (estimated). Measured word by word on 24 October 2026, with the open codes and doubtful words as holes, those five build coherent sentences but read at 86.7–92.5% of their words (R7487 90.1%, R7491 92.5%, R7494 86.7%, R7495 92.4%, R7499 91.7%), under the 95% a complete reading needs.

05 The codes

Three layers sit beside the letter table: three-digit numbers for persons, courts and words (77 distinct, 502 occurrences), a few two- and one-digit numbers (83, “5”, “3.”, “2.”), and dotted letters before codes and runs (o., b., L., d., e., f., g., c., m., p., oo, ooo; 134 occurrences). The recipient settled the last two layers himself in N. 32: he wrote der over “o.” and von Kayser over “5 83”. The dotted letters are therefore a code for small words, not nulls.

CodenValueGradeEvidence
o.34derCthe recipient’s gloss; “Es hat 189 o. 187 heute … schreiben wollen”, where the writer first wrote der and struck it
5, 833, 9von, der KayserCthe recipient’s gloss; “83 ist wegen 204 also zu timide”, “f. 83 gar sehr wohl angesehen”
L., f., b.11, 13, 18und, bey, von demM, M, I“b. 154 L. 166 zugleich unterschrieben”; “ob 189 f. 254 reussire”; “b. 187 bin ersuchet worden”
18956Baron Schenck, the addresseeM“einem diplomate vor 189”, “das diploma des 189”, the genealogy of “der Schenckisch-Nideggischen familie”; “des 189 zugestossenen Unpäßlichkeit”
15042King Augustus IIM“in favorem 150”; “dass 150 nichts von Schweden zu erwarten habe”; “Ministres 3. 150”
1009the imperial courtM“das interieur von 100”; “bei 100 mächtige grosse desordre”
2045SwedenM“83 ist wegen 204 also zu timide”; “Resolutionen, so etwann 204 eine jalousie verursachen könnten”
509, 513, 515, 519, 52417Friede, Ordre, Progressen, Reise, UrlaubM“in pto des 509, worauf nunmehro gedacht werden will”; “die 513 du cabinet”; “weiter glückliche 515”; “seine 519 auf morgen oder übermorgen festgestellet”; “beÿ 100 524 nehmen”
502, 518, 5219Abreise, Recommendation, ResolutionIbounded by the alphabetical order of the 500s, which rise from F (509) through O, P, R to U (524), and fitting their sentences

The persons are the gap. Their roles are clear from a second reading of every sentence that holds a code: 187 (104 times) is the writer’s principal in Vienna, in the King’s service and paid by bill of exchange; 181 (67) is the King’s man at the imperial court, with a secretary, couriers to 150 and rheumatism in his arm; 166 a minister at the King’s side; 190 an imperial minister who “nec superiorem, nec parem aut inferiorem vertragen kann” and wants “ein monopolium der affairen” (not Charles XII, who is named in clear in the same letter); 132 and 193 rivals for one post. The 100s and 200s do not follow the alphabet (204 Sweden breaks it), so position bounds nothing, and none of the names written in clear (Wackerbarth, Senfft, Plessen, Heinisch) can be tied to a code.

06 Method

  1. Pulled the metadata of R7460–R7507 through the DECODE API and the images with a logged-in session; no record carries a key or decipherment.
  2. Two transcription passes over the 52 images of the Schenck letters: clear text as far as legible, every cipher number, superscript and docket.
  3. A free homophonic solver (any number to any letter, German n-grams of the 1720s–60s) gave no sense on the 642 numbers then in hand.
  4. The runs end in 19 43, 21 45, 20 44, 19 55, and the frequent numbers cluster. If the homophones are consecutive numbers in alphabetical order, the key is only 23 boundaries. Annealing those boundaries alone against the same model gave blocks of three from a = 7–9 in the best restart (−913 against −1,212 to −1,496 for the others) and, at once, de Baron Kleinburg alhier and correspondenz.
  5. Every run was transcribed again at full resolution against the key: 24 lines corrected, most of them the writer's 31 taken for 21 and his delta-shaped 6 for 0 or 8.
  6. A second pass re-read every sentence that holds a code or a dotted sign (about 90 crops) and the recipient's glosses; the code was then tested for alphabetical order, which holds for the 500s and not for the persons.
  7. Outside help was sought for the names: printed editions, the lists of Saxon envoys at Vienna (Wackerbarth to 1706, Vesnich 1706–08), and the Wiener Diarium of 1706 for dated cribs, which the Austrian National Library serves only behind a bot check. None gave a key or a name.
  8. The keys of the Sieniawski letters once filed with these (Kaczka’s key A, the R7475 table) were scored on the runs and do not fit (−5.6 and −7.0 per character against −2.8 for the block key).

07 What remains uncertain

  • 65 of the 77 three-digit codes, 394 occurrences, nearly all persons: 187, 181, 166, 190, 254, 258, 132, 193, 186 and the singletons. No key is filed with the letters, on DECODE or in print, and the clear text gives roles, not names; the Saxon cipher keys of the period would be in the Hauptstaatsarchiv Dresden.
  • About ninety small word signs (d., e., g., c., m., p., oo, ooo, “3.”, “2.”): articles or pronouns, but no single value fits every occurrence.
  • Eight runs (44 numbers) with no sense, listed above; their digits were checked twice, and each occurs once.
  • Grades: o., 5 and 83 C (the recipient's gloss); 189, 150, 100, 204, L., f. and five words of the 500s M; b., 502, 518, 521 I. The clear text is transcribed only in part ([?] where water damage or the hand defeat it).
  • Who “Sidon” was, and which Schenck: neither is identified. DECODE's “NN z Drezna (jezuita?)” does not fit letters dated from Vienna.

Full texts

The transcription and the decryption of every document on this page, so that the reading can be used and checked. Short files are shown here; all are in the repository.

R7486, N. 18, Vienna, 7 March 1706 decoded, not read

A letter of the numbered series from the Vienna agent signing 'Sidon' to Baron Schenck, chamberlain of Augustus II, at Cracow, 7 March 1706: court news and intrigue in clear German, with the two-digit letter cipher deciphered (in braces) and most three-digit person codes left open.

A mechanical decode with holes in most sentences: shown so that the transcription and the key can be checked and improved, not as a reading.

# R7486 (N. 18), Vienna, 7 March 1706: the agent "Sidon" to Baron Schenck — reading

Rests on: the diplomatic transcription `transcr/R7486.txt` re-checked line by line on the full-resolution DECODE images
(IMG_R7486_I33848_P1-P3, 22 Oct 2026, batch b05; corrections in `R7486_corrections.tsv`), the letter table of `key.py`
for the two-digit runs (the recipient's own superscripts over three runs agree), and the code values of
`codes_key.json`. No contemporary decipherment beyond those superscripts, no clear copy, no edition.

State (22 Oct 2026, batch b05): **decoded, not read**; clean-sentence share of the reading body 0.29 (`research/reading_audit/b05_score.py`); holes: open codes and signs in most sentences. Log: `R7486-R7496_reread_log.md`.

Conventions: `{...}` a code or sign with a value (`{der}` = o., `{und}` = L., `{bey}` = f., `{Baron Schenck}` = 189,
`{König Augustus}` = 150, `{dem Kayser}` = 83, `{Abreise}` = 502); `[187]` an unvalued three-digit code; `<e.>` an
unvalued dotted sign or small code; `[?word]` a word the figures or the hand carry only in part; `[word]` a word
supplied that nothing carries; `[…]` unread. Spelling as written. In the reading, signs are printed without
their dot (`<e>` for "e.") and abbreviations without the abbreviation point, so that the sentence count is not cut at them.

## Reading

Mit dieser Post habe von {Baron Schenck} der [187] abermahls nichts erhallten. Dieser glaubet, [es] sey kein guttes omen alhier durch {der} [190] <g> seine {Abreise} {bey} {König Augustus}; <oj> [?gutswor] [181] {und} [187] laboriren. {Der} Herr Senfft hat gar <3> [181] abgesezt, {und} <e> [190] sich geschlagen. Unterdessen [187] sich nicht erkläret.

Es verlanget [187] sehr zu wissen, ob der [?brieffe] mit voriger Post, welche jetzo [181] an [413] dirigiret worden, richtig werden eingelauffen seÿn. Heute seÿnd Ihro Kh der Prince von Savoyen nach Italien aufgebrochen; ich bin gleich beÿ der […] und habe Sie beÿde zu dero Wagen mit begleitet. Der Herr von Heinisch lässet seine Anliegen beÿ dem {Baron Schenck} auf alle weise recommendiren und glaubt, dass er wohl durch seinen Fleiss und auch {König Augustus} und [181] viel nützliches effectuiret werden solle.

{Der} [190] [?soll] hier viel Cabalen wieder [181] vorgehabt haben, so [187] dessipiret. <3> […] lauffenden […] {bey} [101] wieder <c> Gnade <e> kommen, aber ohne [516]. [183] is[t] {bey} {dem Kayser} gar sehr wohl angesehen, insonderheit <3> [103] sehr estimiret.

Mit nächster Post wird [187] [?übersenden] den Aufsatz des freÿherrlichen memorials zu einem diplomate, vor {Baron Schenck} Herrn Vater zu überschicken, damit, wann solches approbiret, alles [?so viel] eher zu [?Stande] gebracht werden. Es kömmt der [?taxe] selbsten mit [181] der [187] und [?so] vielleicht von [230] beÿ dem Rescript eine moderation zu erhalten. {Der} Reichsvicecantzler so [134] zu notificiren [?vermeinet], dass Er von sich so ob futura […] nicht Ihro Königs […]; [?gehet] der Herr Graff […] darauff von hier als […] an an den König von Schweden.

Ich will hoffen, es werde derselbe nach der Ihm mitgegebenen Instruction, als etwann seine von anderen an Ihm observirten passionen vor Schweden folgen; auf diesen fall verhoffentlich von Ihro Königl [Majestät] ein guter effect [?bei] generositet seÿn möchte. [?Gewünscht] ist es, wann {König Augustus} a bel agio <3> den [508] sich überzeugen können, dass {König Augustus} nichts von Schweden zu erwarten habe.

Unterdessen beÿ [?mehrerer] sicherheit wegen richtig lauffender Posten der {Baron Schenck} der [187] seine sentiments eröffnen wird. Beÿ [166] bittet [187] sehr um pardon, dass Er mit dieser Post nicht seine devoir ein genüg [?leistet]. Er soll mit nächster gewisslich […] Ihm die […] des [136] etwas Zeit weggenommen, welches [187]. Ich melde nur [?committirter] weise {Baron Schenck}, mit sicherheit so viel {der} [190] [?betreffend], bloss zu denen nöthigen mesures zu schreiten; darum alle [?seine] [510] durch [181] und [190] vermeine, dass die Gedancken so geheim gehallten werden, damit <oj> so [181] [?contrair] erfolgen [?werden].

P.S. Dass [190] nec superiorem, nec parem, aut inferiorem vertragen können, gönnen ein monopolium <3> affairen […]. […] seÿ [527] […] haben wolle, als [186] exempel be[zeugt]. Ob es {König Augustus} [?avantage] […], lehret die Zeit.

## Close (dateline and subscription, not scored)

Bleibe, Monsieur, Votre tres humble e tres obeissant [serviteur], Sidon. (Foot: paraph, "d. 7. Marti 1706"; the P.S. below is written in the middle margin.)

## Notes

* Margin of p. 1 (left): "+ ist in Zukunfft 192" (a key note: the sign + is henceforth 192); vertical, beside ll. 20-29:
  "die Expedition b. vor der o. […] 166. recommendiret desto ehester zu überschreiben" (not built into the body).
* `[?gutswor]`: 25 66 61 58 67 47 55 = g u t s w o r, probably "gut[e]s wor[t]" with letters dropped (the writer drops
  letters elsewhere: "übermogen", "contair"); not printed as a word.
* "<3.> […] lauffenden": 60 46 38 37 69 32 27 38 9 64 22 20 19 43 18 20 45 = s o l l w i g l a u f e e n d e n; the
  recipient wrote "a u f" over 9 64 22; a word "zuwiderlauffenden" with letters lost is possible, not printed.
* `[?soll]`: 58 46 37 78 = s o l y (78 written clearly where l needs 37-39).
* `[?contrair]`: 15 48 45 62 7 31 55 = c o n t a i r.
* "an an den König von Schweden": the word is written twice across the line break.

# R7486 (N. 18), Vienna, 7 March 1706: the agent "Sidon" to Baron Schenck — English

Translation of `R7486_reading.md` (22 Oct 2026, batch b05), with the same holes: `[187]` an unvalued code, `<e.>` an
unvalued sign, `[?word]` partly carried, `[word]` supplied, `[…]` unread. The letter stays "decoded, not read".

## Translation

With this post [187] has again received nothing from {Baron Schenck}. He believes it is no good omen here, through {the} [190] <g.> his {departure} {to} {King Augustus}; <oj> [?good word] [181] {and} [187] are working. {The} Herr Senfft has even <3.> deposed [181], {and} <e.> [190] has gone over. Meanwhile [187] has not declared himself.

[187] very much wants to know whether the [?letters] by the last post, which [181] has now directed to [413], have arrived safely. Today His Highness the Prince of Savoy left for Italy; I was right at the […] and accompanied them both to their carriage. Herr von Heinisch commends his concerns to {Baron Schenck} in every way, and believes that much that is useful should be achieved through his diligence and also {King Augustus} and [181].

{The} [190] is said to have [?had] many cabals here against [181], which [187] dissipated. <3.> […] running […] {at} [101] again <c.> grace <e.> come, but without [516]. [183] is very well regarded {by} {the Emperor}, and <3.> [103] particularly esteemed.

By the next post [187] will [?send] the draft of the baronial memorial for a diploma, to be sent to {Baron Schenck}'s father, so that once it is approved everything can be [?brought about] the sooner. The [?tax] itself comes with [181] the [187], and perhaps [230] can obtain a moderation in the rescript. {The} Imperial Vice-Chancellor [134] [?means] to notify that he of himself so whether futura […] not His Majesty's […]; Count […] [?goes] from here as […] to the King of Sweden.

I will hope that he will follow the instruction given him, as perhaps his passions for Sweden observed in him by others; in that case, it is hoped, a good effect from His Royal [Majesty] [?in] generosity might follow. It is [?to be wished] that {King Augustus} could at leisure <3.> convince the [508] that {King Augustus} has nothing to expect from Sweden.

Meanwhile, with [?more] security through regularly running posts, [187] will open his sentiments to {Baron Schenck}. [187] begs [166]'s pardon that he does not do his duty with this post. He shall with the next, certainly. Today the […] of [136] took some of his time, which [187]. I report to {Baron Schenck} only [?as commissioned], as far as {the} [190] is concerned, to proceed to the necessary measures with security; therefore all [?his] [510] through [181] and [190] think that the thoughts are kept so secret that <oj> so [181] [?contrary] follow. I remain, Monsieur, your very humble and very obedient [servant], Sidon.

P.S. That [190] can bear neither superior, nor equal, nor inferior, grant a monopoly <3.> affairs […]. […] be [527] […] wants to have, as [186]'s example [shows]. Whether it [?is to the advantage] of {King Augustus}, time will tell.

# R7487, Vienna, 13 March 1706: the agent "Sidon" to Baron Schenck — reading

Rests on: the decryption `reading/R7487.txt` (the transcription `transcr/R7487.txt` of 9 Oct 2026 with the codes and
runs resolved), the letter table of `key.py` for the runs and the code values of `codes_key.json`. Written 23 Oct 2026
(r2m, Claude Opus 5.5) under TRANSLATE_BRIEF: only word division, capitals and punctuation normalised; no letter
changed, no word added. Not re-read on the images in this pass (the audit of 22 Oct counted the letter read, grade B).
No contemporary decipherment, clear copy or edition.

The letter is clear German except two short runs of the two-figure letter cipher (P1 l.5 and l.16) and the
three-figure codes. Reading order P1 → P3a → P3b → P2.

Conventions: `{...}` a code or sign with a value (`{König Augustus}` = 150, `{Baron Schenck}` = 189, `{bey}` = f.,
`{der Kayser}` = 83); `[181]` an unvalued code; `<d>` an unvalued sign (printed without its dot); `[word]` supplied; `[?word]` partly carried (doubtful in the
transcription); `[…]` unread (a word left `[?]` in the transcription, a paper hole, or a run that gives no word).
Spelling as written.

## Reading

Es ist heute die Post abermahl wegen des anhalltenden Schnee- und Regenwetters sehr spat angekommen. Nichts destoweniger habe [181] alles vorgestellet, was Sie wegen des […] […] gemeldet; Sie hatten aber bereits ein Copie darvon. Dem [187] ist nichts bekümmerlicher, alß dass zwischen [181] und [190] die Missverstand nicht mehr ab als zu nimbt, und weiss [187] kaum, glaubet vermögend zu seyn, solchen ein temperament oder hülffe zu finden, bevorab da es scheinet, ob trage auch {König Augustus} kein besondere confidence mehr gegen [190]. Unterdessen einige […]liche proben sich eüssern wegen der […] […] <d> [314] […]mann {bey} {der Kayser}. Kein […] […] positis ponendis.

Mann trägt […] mit einer Zeitung, ob habe der H General Rhenschild dem Könige von Schweden vorgeschlagen, das Zubezeugung der Grossmüthigkeit dem Königl Prinzen kein Regiment und jedes […] gefangen werden freÿ geben, auch solchen durch die übrig gefangene completiren lassen und Ihn zu einer Leibguardie […], darbeÿ aber bedingen solle, dass dieser niemahls wider [?wieder] den König von Schweden dienen solle. Was an diesem [?Regiment] und auf was absichten es geschehe, wird die Zeit die beste [?solution] davon geben.

Der [181] verwundert sich sehr, dass in einiger Zeit von [186] nichts eingelauffen, darunter [187] remonstriret: Es seÿ der [186] doch, dass wann Ihm seine Ordre es auch seÿ die Antwort schuldig bleibe, so unterliesse Er [?ferner] zu schreiben. Er meinte aber, [181] dem [186] waren seine überhäuffte occupationes bekannt, und solchem nach, dass selber mit Ihm eine distinction gegen andere correspondencen zu machen. Ich sehe es vor […]sam an, der [186] möchte etwas von dem rigore seines sonst nicht unrechten principij zu remittiren. Gewiss ist es, dass die [?Unterschrift] des Herren Landgraffens von [?Hessen]berg Ihm viel, die publica und Königl Mayt dienste aber noch mehr Zeit wegnehmen; darbeÿ aber auch dem und wann sich eine […]lichkeit zeiget, welche man zuvor so viel möglich [?vorbiegt], und dardurch geniesst die kräffte nicht verbessert werden.

Die Correspondentz mit {Baron Schenck} wird nunmehro […] besser sich einrichten lassen. Die briefe durch den Courrier seÿnd alle eingelauffen, und hat [181] ebenfalls dieser tagen einen an {König Augustus} spediret, auf dessen retour [181] mit grossem verlangen wartet. Ob der [181] retour noch vor dem ferien zu hoffen, zweiffelt [187], und darff sich dieser nicht unterstehen, ferner remonstrationen dass selber zu thun, weilen [187] persuadirt ist, derjenige seÿ sein feind, der Ihm ijzo dargegen rathe, bis Er von {König Augustus} auf seine repraesentation antwort habe.

## Close (not scored)

Womit verharre, Monsieur, Votre tres humble tres obeiss. Serv. [signature, read "Sidon"?]. W[ien] d. 13 Mart 1706.

## Notes

* P1 l.5 "des 37 15 19 61 28 […]": the run gives l c e t h under the table, no word (one of the eight senseless runs of
  NOTES, digits confirmed on the images 9 Oct); a word left `[?]` follows.
* P1 l.16-17: the run "32 43 64 9 43 | 6 49 59 32 | 4 [paper hole] 4" gives i n u a n · p s i and is broken by a hole in
  the paper; then "d. 314", "[?]mann f. 83". Read as holes.
* "f. 83" = bey + der Kayser (both valued from the recipient's glosses and context, grade C/M).

# R7487, Vienna, 13 March 1706: the agent "Sidon" to Baron Schenck — English

Translation of `R7487_reading.md` (23 Oct 2026, r2m, Claude Opus 5.5), the reading of the decryption `R7487.txt`
(clear German with two short cipher runs and three-figure codes). Same holes: `[187]` an unvalued code, `<d>` an
unvalued sign, `[?word]` partly carried, `[…]` unread. Codes with a value in braces, as in the reading.

## Translation

The post again arrived very late today because of the continuing snow and rain. Nevertheless [181] has presented everything that you reported concerning the […] […]; but they already had a copy of it. Nothing troubles [187] more than that the misunderstanding between [181] and [190] does not lessen but grows, and [187] hardly knows, hardly believes himself able, to find any temperament or remedy for it, especially as it seems that {King Augustus} too no longer has any particular confidence in [190]. Meanwhile some […] proofs are showing themselves concerning the […] […] <d> [314] […]mann {with} {the Emperor}. No […] […] positis ponendis.

A newspaper is going about saying that General Rehnskiöld has proposed to the King of Sweden that, as a token of magnanimity, he should set free for the royal prince one regiment and each […] taken prisoner, have it brought to strength from the remaining prisoners and [make] it into a life guard for him […], but should stipulate that it never serve against the King of Sweden [?again]. What there is in this [?regiment], and with what intentions it is done, time will give the best [?solution] of it.

[181] is much surprised that nothing has come in from [186] for some time, on which [187] remonstrated: it is [186]'s way that when he owes an answer, even to his own orders, he stops writing [?further]. But [181] thought that [186] knew of his overwhelming occupations, and that accordingly he should make a distinction with him over other correspondences. I consider it […] that [186] should remit something of the rigour of his otherwise not unjust principle. It is certain that the [?signing] of the Landgrave of [?Hessen]berg takes much of his time, and public business and His Royal Majesty's service still more; and besides, when some […] appears, which one forestalls beforehand as far as possible, and the strength thereby enjoyed cannot be improved.

The correspondence with {Baron Schenck} can now be arranged […] better. The letters by the courier have all come in, and [181] has likewise sent one to {King Augustus} in these days, for whose return [181] waits with great longing. [187] doubts whether [181]'s return can be hoped for before the holidays, and he dare not venture to make further remonstrances, since [187] is persuaded that whoever now advises him against it is his enemy, until he has an answer from {King Augustus} to his representation.

## Close

With which I remain, Monsieur, your very humble, very obedient servant [signature, "Sidon"?]. Vienna, 13 March 1706.

# R7488 (N. 15), Vienna, 27 March 1706: the agent "Sidon" to Baron Schenck — reading

Rests on: `transcr/R7488.txt` re-checked line by line on the full-resolution DECODE images (IMG_R7488_I33856_P1, P3,
22 Oct 2026, batch b05; corrections in `R7488_corrections.tsv`), the letter table of `key.py` for the two-digit runs and
the code values of `codes_key.json`. No contemporary decipherment, clear copy or edition.

State (22 Oct 2026, batch b05): **decoded, not read**; clean-sentence share of the reading body 0.556 (`research/reading_audit/b05_score.py`); holes: open codes in two of nine sentences, one run without sense. Log: `R7486-R7496_reread_log.md`.

Conventions: `{...}` a code or sign with a value (`{der}` = o., `{Baron Schenck}` = 189, `{König Augustus}` = 150,
`{dem kaiserlichen Hof}` = 100, `{Reise}` = 519, `{Urlaub}` = 524, `{Resolution}` = 521, grade I); `[187]` an unvalued
code; `<e.>` an unvalued sign; `[?word]` partly carried; `[word]` supplied; `[…]` unread. Spelling as written. In the reading, signs are printed without
their dot (`<e>` for "e.") and abbreviations without the abbreviation point, so that the sentence count is not cut at them.

## Reading

Es seÿnd unterschiedene Posten verstrichen, dass von {Baron Schenck} weder [181] noch [187] keine Zeÿlen erhallten. Darum [?kommt] man nicht weiss, was daraus zu schliessen. Es hat mir auch [187] aufgetragen, dessen Zeilen mit verlohrener weisse zu schreiben, weilen Er in der persuasion, das auch solche verlohren gehen machten.

Es seÿ [181] [?die] präsumption, ob wir [?braucht] [254] <e> [352] müsse zu [?unterbrechen] {der} correspondenz so no[ch] [526] in Zukunfft zu notiren: etwas müsse entamiret haben. Der [187] hat mir gesagt, ich solle wenig worte schreiben, bis man wegen sicherheit der briefe mehr versichert wäre.

Und [181] lassen {Baron Schenck} melden, wie seine {Reise} auf morgen oder übermorgen festgestellet, und wolle Er nur noch beÿ {dem kaiserlichen Hof} {Urlaub} nehmen; denn Plessen aber eine {Resolution}, woraus [?scheinet], [?dass] von {König Augustus} Ihm eine convoy […]. Keine antworten [?können], denn allzeit wird [187] mit {König Augustus} ein mehreres melden.

## Close (dateline and subscription, not scored)

Ich aber verharre, Monsieur, Votre tres humble Serviteur, Sidon. (Foot of p. 2: paraph, "d. 27. Mart 1706".)

## Notes

* "dei präsumption": the run is 16 19 31 = d e i (first read 16 19 21 = d e e); the article "die" with the vowels
  transposed, printed `[?die]`.
* "correspondenz": 13 46 55 57 20 59 49 46 45 18 21 45 79 = c o r r e s p o n d e n z.
* "morgen oder übermorgen": 40 46 55 25 19 43 / 46 17 56 / 64 10 21 57 42 46 27 21 45 = morgen odr ubermogen (letters
  dropped by the writer).
* "Plessen": 49 37 20 58 59 21 44 = p l e s s e n, a person (probably of the Danish-Mecklenburg Plessen family); the
  sentence lacks its verb.
* "eine convoy": 20 32 44 20 15 46 45 67 48 77 = e i n e c o n w o y.
* "ver-/sichert" is divided between the foot of p. 1 ("ver-", read "wegen[?]" before) and the head of p. 2.

# R7488 (N. 15), Vienna, 27 March 1706: the agent "Sidon" to Baron Schenck — English

Translation of `R7488_reading.md` (22 Oct 2026, batch b05), with the same holes: `[187]` an unvalued code, `<e.>` an
unvalued sign, `[?word]` partly carried, `[word]` supplied, `[…]` unread.

## Translation

Several posts have gone by in which neither [181] nor [187] has received any lines from {Baron Schenck}. Therefore one does not know what to conclude from it. [187] has also charged me to write his lines in a lost manner, since he is persuaded that such letters too have been made to go astray.

[181] is of [?the] presumption whether we [?need] [254] <e.> [352] must [?interrupt] {the} correspondence so still [526] to note in future: something must have broken it open. [187] told me I should write few words until one were more assured of the safety of the letters.

And [181] has {Baron Schenck} told that his {journey} is fixed for tomorrow or the day after, and he wants only to take {leave} at {the Imperial court}; for Plessen however a {resolution}, from which […] from {King Augustus} him an escort […]. [?Can] give no answers, for [187] will always report more with {King Augustus}.

I remain, Monsieur, your very humble servant, Sidon.

R7489_reading.md

# R7489 (N. 27), Vienna, 8 May 1706: the agent "Sidon" to Baron Schenck — reading

Rests on: `transcr/R7489.txt` re-read line by line on the full-resolution DECODE images (IMG_R7489_I33857_P1-P3,
22 Oct 2026, batch b05; the margin of P2 rotated; corrections in `R7489_corrections.tsv`) and the code values of
`codes_key.json`. The letter is almost all clear German; it has no letter-table run, only three-digit codes. Its holes
were illegible words of a faded, water-stained hand, most of them now read. No contemporary decipherment, clear copy or
edition.

State (22 Oct 2026, batch b05): **decoded, not read**; clean-sentence share of the reading body 0.545 (`research/reading_audit/b05_score.py`); holes: open codes and clear-text numerals counted as holes (0.636 with the numerals counted as words); a few faded lines. Log: `R7486-R7496_reread_log.md`.

Conventions: `{...}` a code with a value (`{der}` = o., `{Baron Schenck}` = 189, `{König Augustus}` = 150); `[187]` an
unvalued code; `[?word]` partly carried; `[word]` supplied; `[…]` unread. Spelling as written. In the reading, signs are printed without
their dot (`<e>` for "e.") and abbreviations without the abbreviation point, so that the sentence count is not cut at them.

## Reading

Des [166] reconvalescence hat den [187] einen grossen Kummer benommen; Er bittet darüber seine Hertzl gratulation abzustatten. Wegen der Lehns affaire seÿnd heute [?zweÿ] Königl Handbriefe an Ihro Kayserl Mayt eingelauffen, und soll nunmehro alles vollends auf beste beobachtet werden, wovon heute abermahl ein allergnädigstes Decret ergangen, dessen Verzögerung [190] widerumb auf sich genommen; ich weiss nicht, wohin Er solche diligiret. Was von dem Graffen Lagnasco mit heütiger Post eingelauffen, wird hierbeÿ geschickt.

{Der} [562] [181] träget [187] grosses Verlangen aus vielen hochwichtigen Ursachen. Die particularitäten von der jüngsten action beÿ Montechiaro seÿn nunmehro auch eingelauffen, und haben 6000 teutsche gegen 25 m Frantzosen doch etliche Stunden lang tapffern Stand gehallten und von denen Frantzosen 3 Brigadiers, 400 Officirer und über 600 gemeine niedergemacht, endlich aber gar das Feld und Feldstücke sambt einiger bagage lassen müssen. Die Klage fallet auf den Graffen von [?Reventau], so nunmehro Gener Major worden; ob Er beÿ der [?verantwortung] […] wird, [ist] sehr [?zweiffelhafft].

[…] und Hungarn wollen die friedens aspecten noch immer gar favorable continuiren, und machen sich viele die hoffnung, weilen Ihro Kayserl Mayt dem Ragotzkÿ erlaubet, einen Courrier zu Ihren Gemahl zu thun, dass solche nicht ohne glückliche wirckung seÿn werden. Aus Spanien lauffen die Nachrichten so different, dass man fast nicht weiss, was zu glauben; erwarten […] ein Courrierer sich einfinden. Europa hat wohl noch nie in grösseren Revolution als ijzo sich befunden. Dennemarck [?solle] nun [?…] besag mit Schweden wegen der [?Holsteinischen] Angelegenheit in Missverständnüss gerathen; allein […] maximen hege, als beÿ der alea belli sein Vermögen aufzuwagen.

Da wann {Baron Schenck} die Commission wegen [131] verlangten Bücher aufgetragen, belobt [187] und [?bittet um] Nachricht, weilen Er derselben gefraget worden. Ein anderes ist wegen heütiger Wiener Verrichtung mir ohnmöglich, anders dass ich noch hinzusetzen, wie die Patente wegen der Acht-erklärung wider denen Gebrüdern von Churfürsten zu Cölln und Baÿern zu verkauffen sehr scharff verboten worden; doch habe ich endlich, [?wiewohl] […] eine Stunde nach […], auf die Post dem Herrn Graffen v Wackerb abgegeben, bereits ein Exemplar erhallten, so ein Vertrauter beÿzulegen wollen; [?es] durch wunderliche Gelegenheit herein gebracht, […] [?also] […] {König Augustus} noch nicht [?hat].

Königl. […] werde, doch hoffet {Baron Schenck} […], seÿ es nach beliebigen {König Augustus} Vergnügen.

## Close (dateline and subscription, not scored)

Ich verbleibe […] gehorsamster Sidon. W[ien] d. 8. Maj 1706. (Written up the left margin of p. 4.)

## Notes

* The battle is Calcinato (19 April 1706, near Montichiari), where Reventlow's imperial corps was beaten by Vendôme;
  "Reventau" is printed `[?Reventau]` because the name is faded.
* "6000 teutsche gegen 25 m.": the "25" is overlined with a small m (25,000), not a cipher.
* The code on P2 l.4 is written 131 (the earlier transcription had 181).
* "[?bittet um]" (P2 l.5-6): "belobt 187 und Nachricht" lacks a verb; the bracketed words are supplied in part.
Translation

R7489_en.md

# R7489 (N. 27), Vienna, 8 May 1706: the agent "Sidon" to Baron Schenck — English

Translation of `R7489_reading.md` (22 Oct 2026, batch b05), with the same holes: `[187]` an unvalued code, `[?word]`
partly carried, `[word]` supplied, `[…]` unread.

## Translation

[166]'s recovery has taken a great sorrow from [187]; he asks to offer his heartfelt congratulations on it. In the fief affair [?two] royal autograph letters reached His Imperial Majesty today, and everything is now to be observed in the best way, on which a most gracious decree was again issued today, whose delay [190] once more took upon himself; I do not know to what end he pressed it. What came in from Count Lagnasco by today's post is sent herewith.

{The} [562] [181] [187] has great desire for many very important reasons. The particulars of the latest action at Montechiaro have now also arrived: 6,000 Germans held a brave stand against 25,000 French for several hours and cut down 3 brigadiers, 400 officers and over 600 common soldiers of the French, but in the end had to leave even the field and the field-pieces with some baggage. The blame falls on Count [?Reventau], who has now become a major general; whether he will […] at the [?inquiry] [is] very [?doubtful].

[…] and Hungary want to keep the prospects of peace favourable, and many hope, since His Imperial Majesty has allowed Rákóczi to send a courier to his wife, that this will not be without a happy effect. From Spain the news runs so differently that one hardly knows what to believe awaiting […] the arrival of a courier. Europe has surely never been in a greater upheaval than now. Denmark [?is said to] have fallen into misunderstanding with Sweden over the [?Holstein] affair; but […] holds maxims as to stake its fortune on the hazard of war.

When {Baron Schenck} gave the commission for the books [131] wanted, [187] praises it and [?asks for] news, since he was asked about it. Another thing is impossible for me because of today's business in Vienna, except to add that the patents of the ban against the brothers, the Electors of Cologne and Bavaria, have been very strictly forbidden to be sold; but in the end, [?although] […] an hour after […], I handed one at the post to Count Wackerbarth, and have already received a copy, which a confidant wanted to enclose; [?it was] brought in by a strange chance, […] [?so] […] {King Augustus} does not yet [?have].

Royal […] will be, but {Baron Schenck} hopes […], be it at {King Augustus}'s pleasure. I remain […] most obedient Sidon. Vienna, 8 May 1706.

R7490, N. 28, Vienna, 12 May 1706 decoded, not read

A letter of the numbered series from the Vienna agent signing 'Sidon' to Baron Schenck, chamberlain of Augustus II, at Cracow, 12 May 1706: court news and intrigue in clear German, with the two-digit letter cipher deciphered (in braces) and most three-digit person codes left open.

A mechanical decode with holes in most sentences: shown so that the transcription and the key can be checked and improved, not as a reading.

# R7490 (N. 28), Vienna, 12 May 1706: the agent "Sidon" to Baron Schenck — reading

Rests on: `transcr/R7490.txt` re-read line by line on the full-resolution DECODE images (IMG_R7490_I33858_P1-P5,
22 Oct 2026, batch b05; a contrast-enhanced crop for the water-stained foot of p. 2; corrections in
`R7490_corrections.tsv`), the letter table of `key.py` for the runs and the code values of `codes_key.json`. No
contemporary decipherment, clear copy or edition.

State (22 Oct 2026, batch b05): **decoded, not read**; clean-sentence share of the reading body 0.551 (`research/reading_audit/b05_score.py`); holes: runs of open codes and signs. Log: `R7486-R7496_reread_log.md`.

Conventions: `{...}` a code or sign with a value (`{der}` = o., `{bey}` = f., `{von dem}` = b., grade I, `{und}` = L.,
`{der kaiserliche Hof}` = 100, `{König Augustus}` = 150, `{der Kayser}` = 83, `{Schweden}` = 204, `{Baron Schenck}` =
189, `{Abreise}` = 502, `{Friede}` = 509, `{Recommendation}` = 518); `[187]` an unvalued code; `<e>` an unvalued

## Reading

Die Campagne lässet sich vor der gerechten Partheÿ so wohl in Italien als an dem Ober-Rhein gar schlecht und sehr contraire an. Ausser allen Zweiffel wird Ihnen schon bekannt seÿn, dass Landau mit 80 Bataillons und 140 Esquadrons berennet; und weillen es weder mit nothdürfftiger Besatzung noch mit denen andern unentbehrlichen Requisitis aus einer denen Teutschen so fatalen und nunmehr fast incorrigiblen Sicherheit gebührend nicht versehen, der tapffere General Graff von Thüngen sich auch nicht darinnen befindet, so wäre an dessen Verluste fast nicht mehr zu zweiffeln, da wann Haguenau und Drusenheim zugleich verlohren. Der gute Herr Graff v Wackerbart ist derhalben sehr bestürtzt, weillen der beste theil seines Regiments in Hagenau lieget und beÿ der Eroberung die armen Leüte zu Kriegs-Gefangenen gemacht werden müssten.

Aus Spanien wollen noch viel gute hoffnungen gegeben werden, dass man sich wegen Barcellona keines Unfalls zu besorgen haben dürffte, da man glaubt, dass die Frantzosen solche belagerung ehest aufzuheben durch die grosse Ohnmacht der Engel- und Holländer dürfften gezwungen werden. Und wollen bereits einige Nachricht haben, ob habe der Ritter Leake den Graffen Tholouse von Barcellona gäntzlich zurückgeschlagen. Des Königs Carls grosse frommigkeit und männliche tugend [?standhafft] macht allhier beÿ jedermann eine vortreffliche impression, es könnte demselben an Göttlicher Gnade und daher fliessenden glücklichen Successen nicht fehlen, bevorab da Er beÿ allen seinen Vornehmen eine gantz ungemeine Resignation in den Göttlichen willen zu seiner Hauptmaxime führete.

## Notes

* "alles": [9] 38 39 19 59 = (a) l l e s; the first sign is y-shaped.
* "Ogilwy": 46 25 31 37 67 76 = o g i l w y (the Russian general Ogilvy).
* "de Baron Kleinburg alhier": 16 19 ~~40~~ 10 7 55 46 45 34 37 20 31 43 10 64 55 25 7 37 29 32 19 57 = d e (40 struck)
  b a r o n k l e i n b u r g a l h i e r.
* "malewoli": 40 7 37 19 67 46 37 31 = m a l e w o l i (malevoli, the ill-wishers).
* "a[u]s Muscau": 8 oo 59 42 64 59 14 9 66 = a [oo] s m u s c a u; the u of "aus" written as two plain o.
* "sich deshalber bemühen": 59 31 13 28 17 21 60 29 9 37 11 21 55 12 18 42 64 29 20 45 = s i c h d e s h a l b e r b
  d m u h e n (18 written where e needs 19-21).
* "confidence": 13 47 45 22 31 18 19 45 15 21; "einen": 19 31 43 20 44; "avisen": 7 67 31 59 20 45; "ihm": 31 28 41.

* "cauta salutaris": the writer has "Cives salutaris"; the proverb's first word is read from the hand as written and
  is not certain.

# R7490 (N. 28), Vienna, 12 May 1706: the agent "Sidon" to Baron Schenck — English

Translation of `R7490_reading.md` (22 Oct 2026, batch b05), with the same holes: `[187]` an unvalued code, `<e>` an
unvalued sign, `[?word]` partly carried, `[word]` supplied, `[…]` unread. The letter stays "decoded, not read".

## Translation

The campaign looks very bad and very contrary for the just party, both in Italy and on the Upper Rhine. You will no doubt already know that Landau has been invested with 80 battalions and 140 squadrons; and since, out of a security so fatal to the Germans and now almost incorrigible, it is duly provided neither with a needful garrison nor with the other indispensable requisites, and the brave General Count Thüngen is not in it either, its loss would hardly be in doubt any more, since Haguenau and Drusenheim are lost at the same time. The good Count Wackerbarth is therefore much dismayed, since the best part of his regiment lies in Haguenau and the poor men would be made prisoners of war at its capture.

From Spain much good hope is still given that no misfortune need be feared for Barcelona, since it is believed that the French will be forced to raise the siege very soon through the great weakness of the English and Dutch. And some claim to have news that Sir John Leake has entirely beaten back the Count of Toulouse from Barcelona. King Charles's great piety and [?steadfast] manly virtue make an excellent impression on everyone here; he could not lack divine grace and the happy successes flowing from it, especially since in all his undertakings he made an altogether uncommon resignation to the divine will his chief maxim.

As for General von Gross's blow at Radonisky, it is [?almost] impossible to carry it further in the situation of the war, [?especially] […] which at the […] so [?principal] […] not […] more […] on the […] and […] will give occasion for a salvation. Besides, Glogau may not be in a state of defence such that, under this pretext, K 6 could put men out of it in order to preserve it. Here they also had an eye on Jauer or Schweidnitz, in case this duke [and] the general should find it more convenient.

This I can solemnly assure you, that {the Imperial court} is eager to do all that is possible in favour of {King Augustus}; but {the Emperor} is so timid because of {Sweden}, which is to be put down to its inwardly confused state and exhausted strength. Tomorrow too he gave [187] a warning, [?a] letter before his [?hand], to attend to everything except [134], since the latter wanted a great counterpart from {Sweden}, from a correct answer <d> {the} {departure} [181] <e> [340]. That can easily be looked into. For a long time {the} [187] has, from reliable persons, weighed whether such justice [208] [207] <m> a {peace} would be worked for. {The} [187] […] all together […] [?everything] <1> <e> to put off [181]'s {departure}, and I wanted only to report it to {Baron Schenck} in advance, so that he might conduct himself the more cautiously everywhere.

[258] also mentioned to [187] in a conversation these days how {the} Ogilvy henceforth [452] […] [208] much dispute, since <3> has much [528] <p> of Baron Kleinburg here; {the} but reveals everything [207] [525]. Whether it comes from passions, <3> [452] seem not well affected, will be seen {at} {the} {departure} [181] <e> [349]. [?Indispensable] is [181]'s {recommendation} <d> [340], since [187] [notes that] some of the still well-affected ministers, like {the} malevoli, scheme very busily against you; they also wanted to draw [190] to them, whom however [187] brought to better thoughts, and also thwarted his other designs as far as he could, so that their intrigues will, it is hoped, not succeed. […]

<p> to undertake to speak to [258]. {The} said <3> [289] [?be-] <ooo> disturbed; but <1> […] written to him <oj> from [?Moscow]. He believed, however, <ooo> [206] might take trouble about it. {The} [187] therefore asked only to report it to {Baron Schenck}, so that one might give some thought to it if anything further could be gone into, since it seems to him quite incredible, as a rumour that is spread, which should trouble {King Augustus}. Meanwhile [187] holds that such things are not to be thrown to the wind: Cives [cauta?] salutaris: wholesome and circumspect distrust is the mother of happiness. Earlier it seems as if {the} [187] also attended to one thing and another, which so with him <oo> confidence so [?no] [?is], to undertake through [528] very […] held <3> <1> […] [187], but [?will] be [?shall]. For which reason [187] will be vigilant.

Afterwards [28] <o> [61] <3> have [526] <d> [341] {and} may be dedicated to him with avisos <3> without ground. But since in such matters […] […], [187] holds <ooo> that the earlier report will be seen, whose appointment Count Wackerbarth claims. And it is certain too that the Imperial Chancery would make many more difficulties if it did not receive [?assurance] from the gentlemen [?some] of His Imperial Majesty because of His Royal Majesty, our most gracious lord. […] more in future. Since at such occurrences people […] the fatalities so great [?haste] […] no moment of time [?more] catch. [187] asks to present his most dutiful recommendation to [166] in the most obliging terms, and also to congratulate [186] heartily on his arrival in his new function. He would repay this to [187] in every way.

Close: But I remain, Monsieur, your very humble and very obedient servant, Sidon.

# R7491 (N. 31), Vienna, 22 May 1706: the agent "Sidon" to Baron Schenck — reading

Rests on: `transcr/R7491.txt` re-read line by line on the full-resolution DECODE images (IMG_R7491_I33870_P1-P3,
22 Oct 2026, batch b05; corrections in `R7491_corrections.tsv`), the letter table of `key.py` for the runs and the
code values of `codes_key.json`. No contemporary decipherment, clear copy or edition.

State (22 Oct 2026, batch b05): **read**; clean-sentence share of the reading body 0.609 (`research/reading_audit/b05_score.py`); holes: open person codes only. Log: `R7486-R7496_reread_log.md`.

Conventions: `{...}` a code or sign with a value (`{der}` = o., `{der kaiserliche Hof}` = 100, `{Schweden}` = 204,
`{Baron Schenck}` = 189, `{Recommendation}` = 518); `[187]` an unvalued code; `<e>` an unvalued sign; `[?word]` partly
carried; `[word]` supplied; `[…]` unread. Spelling as written. In the reading, signs are printed without their dot
(`<e>` for "e.") and abbreviations without the abbreviation point, so that the sentence count is not cut at them.

## Reading

Die Schreiben des {Baron Schenck} vom 12 und 15 curr oder sub N 27 und 28 seÿnd mit heütiger Post zugleich eingelauffen, und dardurch des [181] Kummer zur genüge gestillet worden. Weillen nun [187] höchst in diesen Geschäfften des [181] zu avanciren zu helffen, gestallten Er noch einigen Abriss solcher begehren will, [?jedoch] die wegen allzu engen Raum beÿ [122] nicht länger bleiben, noch demselben behutsamlich sollen [?zustellen]: so wird Er heüte nur mit wenig worten das nöthigste [?anführen], das übrige aber bis zu künfftiger Post aussetzen müssen.

Was des Herren Gener Radomsky Vorschlag wegen Liegnitz betrifft, will [187] diese ferien selbst untersuchen und seine Gedancken so dann in Lateinischen eröffnen. Vielleicht kann durch des [181] [?gutes] wort auch dissfalls etwas fruchtbarliches effectuiret werden. Dieses ist gewiss, dass {der kaiserliche Hof} ijzo in allen Resolutionen, so etwann {Schweden} eine jalousie verursachen könnten, sehr behutsam sich aufführet, und muss [187] gestehen, dass von Vielen, welchen das interieur von {der kaiserliche Hof} genau bekannt, solche conduite vor ein stück der ijzigen unglücklichen conjuncturen erachten und nicht gäntzlich improbiren.

Was die Brieffe, so an [181] in Zukunfft der {Baron Schenck} ablassen will, anbetrifft, so lässt sich [181] des {Baron Schenck} Vorschlag gar wohl gefallen; nur soll das erste mahl gemeldet werden, was vor eines Nahmens sich {Baron Schenck} beÿ denen Briefen bedienen werde, die von [187] geschehen möchte. Wegen der Schlesischen Angelegenheiten will des [187] Meinung über seinen Vorschlag der [187] mir zuvor aufsetzen und seinem bona fide seine Meinung eröffnen. Herr von Heinisch ist über die gegebene Nachricht von Hertzen erfreuet und erwartet sehnlich die völlige Expedition, [?sich] verpflichtet, nicht allein [181] alle schuldige veneration darneben zu bezeigen und künfftig dero Angelegenheiten ohne einiges salarium zu übernehmen, sondern wird auch seine unverfälschte danckbarkeit beÿ allen occasionen von dem {Baron Schenck} bezeugen.

Der [190] gehet darum eigen <3> [349] <d> [314], will aber seine {Recommendation} <e> [349] auf seinem Versicheren, so aber [181] nicht glaublich hält, welches die Zeit wird eröffnen müssen. {Der} Ban <3> [231] [233] dürffte allerdings noch mit [?verschiedenen] Mitteln auch sich [?trösten], und ist beÿ {der kaiserliche Hof} mächtige grosse desordre, dass man sich sogar selbst der Zeit und deren [?wahren] Momentorum zu bedienen trachtet. Entweder gehet man zu hefftig oder gar zu langsam. Indessen muss die bevorstehende Campagne [?Ausschlag] der Sachen das grösste Licht geben, wovon [187] mit nächsten ein mehreres zu melden verspricht. Gewiss ist es, dass [?man] [?einmahl] <10> [134] <c> seiner proposition allzu hefftige Worte gebrauchte; auch sonsten einige Umstände angemercket werden, so einige apprehension verursachet. Est modus in rebus, fänget sich bey allen Verstand.

Was {Baron Schenck} von denen Wienerischen Zeitung verlangen möchte, [187] gerne wissen; der Mercurius hat allein also so viel Lügen, dass man [?keine] derselben gar nicht achtet; das Diarium ist etwas, aber nicht viel besser, [?…] müssen anckommen: oder dass man einen Extract aus allen andern gleich würdige in solchen enthaltenen [?Unwahrheiten] und in einen Nobel [?…] beÿlegen solle.

Die heute <3> [262] eingelauffene brieffe hat [181] zur Versorgung zu sich genommen und begehret, dass {Baron Schenck} melden solle, wie [190] auf <oo> {Recommendation} <d> [349] gegen [81] fol gedencke. Dass Hagenau übergegangen, ist bereits gemeldet, und seÿn die zu unseren Contingent gehörige troupplen, so darinnen gelegen, zu Kriegs-gefangenen gemacht worden. Durch einen Courierer ist der Entsatz von Barcellona gestern confirmiret worden, dabeÿ noch dieses appendiciret wird, dass Alcantara gleichfalls über gegangen und der Mylord Galloway sich vergewissert, gegen 27 oder 28 April von Madrit zu stehen. Der des Bischoffs zu Raab durchl ist derjenige Pallast, welchen ehedessen die Königin Christina bewohnet, aufs prächtigste meubliret worden, und lässet der Monsignor Kaunitz eine kostbahre Liberei von [?…] verfertigen. Ihro Kaÿserl Maÿst haben sich einige tage zu Laxenburg divertiret, sind aber gestern abends [?unvermuthet] allhier angelanget. […] etwas.

## Close (dateline and subscription, not scored)

Votre tres humble et tres obeiss. Sidon. W[ien] d. 22 Maj 1706. Under the close: "beÿ dem 166. meine gehorsamste
recommendation ..." (one line, faded, not read). Upside down at the head of p. 4, a P.S. of three faded lines ("... 181
...") and a margin note at Hagenau ("wie hier ... Unterschied ...") are not read.

## Notes

* "seine": 58 19 31 43 19 = s e i n e (P3a l.28-P3b l.1; the earlier transcription read the last group 10); a struck
  group follows with "518 f" written above it.
* "Ban": 11 8 45 = b a n (the Ban of Croatia).
* "seiner proposition also sinister": 60 21 33 45 19 55 / 49 56 46 50 47 58 31 61 32 48 44 = s e i n e r / p r o p o s i
  t i o n; "also sinister" is clear.
* "gedencke": 25 19 16 20 45 14 34 20 = g e d e n c k e.
* The closing paragraph's last lines ("Unterdessen ... Vermuthlich ... etwas") are cramped and partly faded; printed
  with `[?]` and `[?…]`.

# R7491 (N. 31), Vienna, 22 May 1706: the agent "Sidon" to Baron Schenck — English

Translation of `R7491_reading.md` (22 Oct 2026, batch b05), with the same holes: `[187]` an unvalued code, `<e>` an
unvalued sign, `[?word]` partly carried, `[word]` supplied, `[…]` unread.

## Translation

{Baron Schenck}'s letters of the 12th and 15th of this month, or under nos. 27 and 28, arrived together by today's post, and [181]'s anxiety has thereby been fully stilled. Since [187] is now most eager to help advance [181] in these affairs, as he still wants some outline of them, [?but] those which, for lack of room at [122], cannot stay there longer nor be handed to him with care: so he will today only [?set out] the most necessary in a few words and must put off the rest until the next post.

As to General Radomsky's proposal concerning Liegnitz, [187] will examine it himself during these holidays and then give his thoughts in Latin. Perhaps something fruitful can be effected in this too through [181]'s [?good] word. This is certain, that {the Imperial court} now behaves very cautiously in all resolutions that might cause {Sweden} jealousy, and [187] must admit that many who know the inside of {the Imperial court} well consider such conduct one of the pieces of the present unhappy conjunctures and do not entirely disapprove of it.

As to the letters which {Baron Schenck} intends to send to [181] in future, [181] is well pleased with {Baron Schenck}'s proposal; only he should be told the first time what name {Baron Schenck} will use in the letters that might come from [187]. On the Silesian affairs, [187] will first draw up for me his opinion of his proposal and give his opinion in good faith. Herr von Heinisch is heartily glad of the news given him and longs for the complete dispatch, [?being] obliged not only to show [181] all due veneration besides, and in future to take on their affairs without any salary, but will also show his unfeigned gratitude to {Baron Schenck} on every occasion.

[190] goes about it himself <3> [349] <d> [314], but wants his {recommendation} <e> [349] on his assurance, which [181] does not find credible; time will have to show it. {The} Ban <3> [231] [233] may well still [?console] himself with [?various] means, and at {the Imperial court} there is mighty great disorder, so that one even tries to make use of time and its [?true] moments. Either one goes too hastily or far too slowly. Meanwhile the coming campaign must throw the greatest light on the [?outcome] of things, of which [187] promises to report more soon. It is certain that [?once] <10> [134] <c> used all too violent words about his proposal; other circumstances too are noticed that cause some apprehension. Est modus in rebus; sense begins with everything.

[187] would like to know what {Baron Schenck} wants of the Viennese newspapers; the Mercurius alone has so many lies that one pays [?none] of them any heed; the Diarium is something, but not much better, [?…] must arrive: or that one should enclose an extract from all the others, equally worthy, in such [?untruths] and in a Nobel [?…].

[181] took into keeping the letters that arrived today <3> [262] and asks {Baron Schenck} to report what [190] thinks about <oo> {recommendation} <d> [349] towards [81] fol. That Hagenau has gone over has already been reported, and the troops of our contingent that lay in it have been made prisoners of war. The relief of Barcelona was confirmed yesterday by a courier, with the addition that Alcántara too has gone over and that Mylord Galloway has made sure of standing before Madrid about 27 or 28 April. The palace of the Bishop of Raab, His Highness, the same that Queen Christina once lived in, has been furnished most splendidly, and Monsignor Kaunitz is having a costly livery made of [?…]. His Imperial Majesty amused himself for some days at Laxenburg, but arrived here [?unexpectedly] yesterday evening. […] something.

Close: your very humble and very obedient Sidon. Vienna, 22 May 1706.

R7492, Vienna, 26 May 1706 decoded, not read

A letter of the numbered series from the Vienna agent signing 'Sidon' to Baron Schenck, chamberlain of Augustus II, at Cracow, 26 May 1706: court news and intrigue in clear German, with the two-digit letter cipher deciphered (in braces) and most three-digit person codes left open.

A mechanical decode with holes in most sentences: shown so that the transcription and the key can be checked and improved, not as a reading.

# R7492, Vienna, 26 May 1706: the agent "Sidon" to Baron Schenck — reading

Rests on: `transcr/R7492.txt` re-read line by line on the full-resolution DECODE images (IMG_R7492_I33874_P1-P3,
22 Oct 2026, batch b05; corrections in `R7492_corrections.tsv`), the letter table of `key.py` for the one run and the
code values of `codes_key.json`. No contemporary decipherment, clear copy or edition.

State (22 Oct 2026, batch b05): **decoded, not read**; clean-sentence share of the reading body 0.371 (`research/reading_audit/b05_score.py`); holes: runs of open codes and signs. Log: `R7486-R7496_reread_log.md`.

Conventions: `{...}` a code or sign with a value (`{der}` = o., `{bey}` = f., `{Baron Schenck}` = 189, `{König
Augustus}` = 150, `{der kaiserliche Hof}` = 100, `{Reise}` = 519, `{Recommendation}` = 518, grade I); `[187]` an
unvalued code; `<c.>` an unvalued sign; `[?word]` partly carried; `[word]` supplied; `[…]` unread. Spelling as written. In the reading, signs are printed without
their dot (`<e>` for "e.") and abbreviations without the abbreviation point, so that the sentence count is not cut at them.

## Reading

Es hat {Baron Schenck} {der} [187] heute sehr weitläufftig schreiben wollen; allein es ist Ihm wenig Zeit übrig geblieben, weilen man auch […] heute <oo> höchst nachtheilige [526] zu kommen, so bishero <c> [157] [527] <d> [349] geschehen. Die Bosheit ist so weit gegangen, dass man [?Ihro] ausführliche Ministres <3> {König Augustus} in einem schreiben âmes ignobles, basses et rampantes, so die mit […] qualitäten beschaffen […], genennet. [181] ist darüber sehr [?empfindlich] und hat zuvor verlanget, {Baron Schenck} Nachricht zu geben, doch dass es biss zu anderen Zeiten menagiret bleiben solle. Es scheinet, ob dürffte einiger massen [258] impliciret werden.

Von heute allerunterthänigst eingereichten Bericht habe bloss dem Herrn Agent Bell in des Cantzleÿ Paquet ein Duplicat zugestellet. [190] wollte, [187] sollte dergleichen an [154] überschreiben, welches Ihm {König Augustus} {bey} seiner {Reise} <3> [355] <d> [349] befohlen, es [?alles] biss auff fernere ordre zu halten. Unterdessen [?M] [181] {der} [187] Nachricht gegeben und von Ihm zur Antwort erhallten, dass <3> {Baron Schenck} vermögen solle, derselbe mit [166] zu wenden, damit durch dessen Vermittelung von {König Augustus} eine ordre an [187] ergehen möchte, wie Er sich verhallten solle. Es wäre aber gut, wann die ordre sogleich an [190] mit geschickt würde, weilen [?dieser] seine {Recommendation} gegen den [?Herrn] nach St Germain [?fortgesetzet].

Von der glücklichen Eroberung Barcellona hat der [187] die felicitation in dem allerhöchsten [?Bericht] angebracht, zugleich auch den Catalogum der neuen Cardinäle. Man glaubt, dass der Reservatus in pectore wohl der Savia seÿn möchte. Wegen des […] angelegenheit will mit nächstem die antwort überschicken; bishero ist die Sache noch nicht zum Vortrage wegen überhäuffter Verrichtungen beÿ der Cantzleÿ gediehen. […] möcht ich wünschen, dass dieselben zu […] beÿ dem […] brauchen könnte.

Wegen des Radoniski wollen Ihro Mt dem Herrn [?Rath] solches bestens mit fleiss anlegen, dass solches noch etwann auf Liegnitz möchte retorquiret werden. Man hat mir gestern en confidence gesagt, wie {der kaiserliche Hof} auch [206] nicht recht vergnügt seÿ und eine declaration deren […] verlanget, ob <3> {der} [500] ein genügen leisten wolle oder nicht; [?worauf] Antwort […] folgen möchte, […] nach dessen […].

Es bittet {Baron Schenck} {der} [187] inständigst umb [166] Titul, welchen Er gern an denselben schreiben und seine felicitation ablegen wollte. Wegen des Hertz von Curland wollen Ihro Mt mit heütiger Post selbst antworten, zu welchem Ende Sie auch das {Baron Schenck} schreiben an sich genommen. […] hat [?dieses] [181] der [187] ein mehreres nicht [?sagen] [?dürffen], dass Er sich nur noch ein etwas patientiren solle, welches [?gleich] beÿ seinem hefftigen desiderio Er gar zu discontentirt sich bezeigen würde, wann die gute hoffnung auf einmahl so gar sehr fallen sollte. Ein mehreres ist heut ohnmöglich.

## Close (dateline and subscription, not scored)

Ich verharre, Monsieur, Votr. tres humb. et tres obeissant Serviteur, Sidon. W[ien] d. 26. Maj 1706.

## Notes

* "seiner": 58 19 31 45 20 55 = s e i n e r.
* 157 on P1 l.6 carries a small b above it (a correction to 156 or a note); read as written.
* "ames ignobles basses et rampantes": the insult in French in the letter quoted, as written.
* "Reservatus in pectore": the cardinal reserved in pectore at the creation of 17 May 1706; "Savia" is as written.
* "Radoniski", "Liegnitz": the Radziejowski/Liegnitz business is not identified further.

# R7492, Vienna, 26 May 1706: the agent "Sidon" to Baron Schenck — English

Translation of `R7492_reading.md` (22 Oct 2026, batch b05), with the same holes: `[187]` an unvalued code, `<c>` an
unvalued sign, `[?word]` partly carried, `[word]` supplied, `[…]` unread.

## Translation

{The} [187] wanted to write to {Baron Schenck} at great length today, but little time was left him, since one also […] today <oo> most damaging [526] to come, as has happened so far <c> [157] [527] <d> [349]. The malice has gone so far that in a letter one called [?His] detailed ministers <3> {King Augustus} "ignoble, base and grovelling souls", which with […] qualities […]. [181] is very [?sensitive] about it and asked beforehand to inform {Baron Schenck}, but that it should be kept quiet until other times. It seems that [258] might to some extent be implicated.

Of today's most humbly submitted report I have handed a duplicate only to Herr Agent Bell in the Chancery packet. [190] wanted [187] to write the same to [154], which {King Augustus} ordered him {at} his {journey} <3> [355] <d> [349], to keep [?everything] until further order. Meanwhile [?M] [181] informed {the} [187] and received from him in answer that <3> {Baron Schenck} should prevail on him to turn to [166], so that through his mediation an order might go from {King Augustus} to [187] on how he should conduct himself. But it would be good if the order were sent at once to [190] as well, since [?he] has [?continued] his {recommendation} to the [?gentleman] at St Germain.

On the happy conquest of Barcelona [187] presented the congratulations in the most exalted [?report], together with the list of the new cardinals. It is believed that the one reserved in pectore may well be Savia. On the […] affair I will send the answer by the next; so far the matter has not come to be presented, because of the heavy business at the Chancery. […] I could wish that they could use […] at the […].

As to Radoniski, His Majesty will set it before Herr [?Rath] with all diligence, so that it might still perhaps be turned back on Liegnitz. I was told in confidence yesterday that {the Imperial court} too is not well pleased with [206] and demands a declaration from them whether <3> {the} [500] will give satisfaction or not; [?whereupon] an answer […] might follow, […] after its […].

{The} [187] earnestly begs {Baron Schenck} for [166]'s title, since he would like to write to him and offer his congratulations. As to the Duke of Courland, His Majesty will answer by today's post himself, to which end he also took {Baron Schenck}'s letter. […] [?has] not [?been able] to tell [181] the [187] more than that he should be patient a little longer, which [?would] make him, with his eager desire, show himself all too discontented if the good hope should fall so very much all at once. More is impossible today.

Close: I remain, Monsieur, your very humble and very obedient servant, Sidon. Vienna, 26 May 1706.

R7493, N. 32, Vienna, 29 May 1706 decoded, not read

A letter of the numbered series from the Vienna agent signing 'Sidon' to Baron Schenck, chamberlain of Augustus II, at Cracow, 29 May 1706: court news and intrigue in clear German, with the two-digit letter cipher deciphered (in braces) and most three-digit person codes left open.

A mechanical decode with holes in most sentences: shown so that the transcription and the key can be checked and improved, not as a reading.

# R7493 (N. 32), Vienna, 29 May 1706: the agent "Sidon" to Baron Schenck — partial reading

Rests on: `transcr/R7493.txt` re-read on the full-resolution DECODE images (IMG_R7493_I33878_P1-P6, 22 Oct 2026, batch
b05), the letter table of `key.py` for the runs and the code values of `codes_key.json`; the recipient's gloss on p. 1
("der" over o., "von Kayser" over "5 83"). Page order: P1, P6a, P6b, P4, P3, P5a, P5b, P2 (ff. 303-306v). No
contemporary decipherment beyond that gloss, no clear copy, no edition. **Stays "decoded, not read"**: the upper halves
of the pages now read, but the water-stained and faded lower halves of ff. 303v-304 and the foot of most pages keep
holes in about half the sentences.

Conventions: `{...}` a code or sign with a value (`{der}` = o., `{von}` = 5, `{dem Kayser}` = 83, `{bey}` = f.,
`{König Augustus}` = 150, `{Baron Schenck}` = 189, `{der kaiserliche Hof}` = 100, `{Schweden}` = 204, `{Reise}` = 519,
`{Abreise}` = 502); `[187]` an unvalued code; `<g>` an unvalued sign; `[?word]` partly carried; `[word]` supplied;
`[…]` unread. Signs are printed without their dot and abbreviations without the point.

## Reading

Des {Baron Schenck} Meinung, dass des [258] Vorgeben und desavantage des [452] aus passionibus herrühre, mag nicht ungegründet seÿn, weillen man in Erfahrung bracht, dass {der} [208] den [258] Ihm vor 2 Monaten {von} {dem Kayser} sein {Reise} zu [?nehmen] [?behülfflich], sich aller wegen Commendirung gelder entschuldiget. Und obschon [451] in nichts [?erst] einig modicum vorgeschlagen, wie der [205] [?welle] zu effectuiren, doch noch nicht völlig vor zulänglich gehalten, [?sondern] vielmehr darüber gestellet, dass [452] davon Ursache seÿ. Es hat [181] unterschiedene Umbstände an [166] zu melden, welche aber {Baron Schenck} noch nicht auf des [187] inständiges [?bitten] Ihm das [166] tittul zugeschicket. Solches den {Baron Schenck} der [187] [?…] gehorsamst nebst seiner Recommendation an [166] darbeÿ part zu geben. Es will doch scheinen, dass man immer näher auf die Spur kommen, aus was vor einer Hand die bisherige mutante [526] aus so [?…] per q […] [343] […] der [?Conat] seÿn soll, ein [?gewesener] diener […] [258], so sich ijzo [?…] aufhalt.

Mit letzterer Post hat an {Baron Schenck} {der} [132] eine französische Relation <3> {dem Kayser} abgestattet, so Er bereits darauff [187] gewiesen. Selbiger hat sich sehr über die libertatem styli aufs empfindlichste passioniret <g> [103] condamniret. Es ist [?wahr], dass dieser kein flattirender Mazarin, sondern ein [?ernster] Richelieu seÿ; [?hingegen] hat er mit dem letzteren den Ruhm, dass […] als das […] […] seÿ, und dass Er keine fanfaronaden als […] liebe, [?hingegen] durch […] fermeté seine Resolutiones durchsetze. Es empfindet auch [181] selbst, dass […] [187] recht habe, und das [103] ein […] von {König Augustus} seÿ; […] dem durch [187] […] alles mistrauen, so einige widrige […] [181] und [103] […], ausgerottet worden. Gewiss ist es, das […] […], und […] realitet verlangen und von Ihm […] satisfaction […] die indiscreten tractements […], die […] machinationes {der} [181] seÿn auf […] {der} {Abreise} [181] [?fast] gäntzlich […] worden.

Er möchte auch allerhand dessein von seiner […] durch {der} [525] <3> {Schweden} <q> {König Augustus} {bey} {dem Kayser} sich eine avantage zu machen [?…], welches [187] auch […] […] contraminiret, […] […] practiciren können, wann Er […] seiner subsistence ein […] erhallten und […] haben. Er hat auch […], dass […] 5 Monate nicht mehr als 400 th erhallten, und […] noch nicht […], wann nicht [181] so […] und Ihm mit 2 […] präterit […] […] Jahr […] geholffen. Die besten […] muss Er […] die […] arcana zu penetriren […], welches […] und Ihm studiret, und so […] secretario erhallten, so perfectissime französisch, italienisch […], zu {König Augustus} […] avantage, allein […] […] wollen […] […] zu {König Augustus} […] ein expediens durch hülffe des [166] auszufinden seÿ, damit [187] in dem [?…] Stande möge {König Augustus} desto bessere dienste zu leisten, gestallten Er der [181] nicht gern disfalls so gar offt incommodiren will.

Wegen [188] wegen des Freÿherrlichen diplomatis und [?…], verspricht auf [?…] beÿgehendes schreiben und den von Ihm verbesserten Aufsatz. Er bittet [187] [?…], nachdem das diploma des {Baron Schenck} […] wird passiret seÿn, [?…] zu remittiren, weillen man aus mangel der Zeit und Leute keine Copiam davon behallten können. Auf [187] heütigen bericht wird […], dass beÿ […] die […] […] als […] und Schweden […]: die […] solle seÿn, wegen der […] […] tumulte allzugrossen und […] submissionen. […] mit […] specialiora: und […] […], dass […] affirmativa & negativa […] werden, […] der [166] […] gestellet […] solle: welches das avantageuse […] éclat in dergl […] profitabler halten […] diejenige, so die Erfahrung […] […] Rathschläge braucht.

Die glückliche Entsetzung von Barcellona continuiret, auch variiren einiger Umbstände, nemblich ob der Conte Toulouse zur See geschlagen, und 2) ob der Duc d'Anjou sich retiriret habe nach Roussillon. Das erste und letztere wird dubiös gemacht und [?…], dass der Toulouse auf ordre von dem französ Hoffe sich ohne Hinderung der Engelländer in den Hafen nach Toulon in Sicherheit begeben; hingegen aber der Duc d'Anjou keinen Weg zu seiner retraite offen gefunden, sondern dergestallt eingeschlossen seÿ, dass Ihm keine retraite zu [?…] ohnmüglich fallen würde. In Hungarn will sich die friedens hoffnung [?vermehren], welchem nach künfftigen Montag sich der Ihro Hochf G der Bischoff von Olmütz samt dem Graffen von Wratislaw Excell nach Pressburg begeben werden, allwo in Zukunfft der […] Congress seÿn soll, zu dem [?…] [?…]. R7493_en.md

# R7493 (N. 32), Vienna, 29 May 1706: the agent "Sidon" to Baron Schenck — English (partial)

Translation of the partial reading `R7493_reading.md` (22 Oct 2026, batch b05), with the same holes: `[187]` an
unvalued code, `<g>` an unvalued sign, `[?word]` partly carried, `[…]` unread. The letter stays "decoded, not read";
only the passages that build sentences are translated, the rest is summarised in brackets.

## Translation

{Baron Schenck}'s opinion, that [258]'s pretext and [452]'s disadvantage come from passions, may not be unfounded, since it has been learned that {the} [208] two months ago [?helped] [258] to make his {journey} {from} {the Emperor}, excusing himself on every count of commission money. And although [451] proposed some small means of effecting what [205] [?wanted], it was not yet held fully sufficient; rather it was put down to [452] being the cause. [181] has various circumstances to report to [166], but {Baron Schenck} has not yet sent him [166]'s title at [187]'s urgent [?request]. [The rest of the page, on the hand that has been opening the letters (a former servant of [258]), is read only in part.]

By the last post {the} [132] sent {Baron Schenck} a French report <3> {to the Emperor}, to which he had already referred [187]. The latter was extremely offended by the freedom of the style <g> [103] condemned. It is [?true] that he is no flattering Mazarin but a [?stern] Richelieu, [and shares with the latter the glory of firmness in his resolutions …]. [181] himself feels that [187] is right and that [103] is a […] of {King Augustus}; all the mistrust that some hostile […] [had sown between] [181] and [103] has been rooted out. [The rest of f. 303v, and f. 304 on [?the agent's] means — five months with no more than 400 thalers — and a secretary perfect in French and Italian, are read only in part.]

[f. 304v:] … an expedient is to be found through [166]'s help, so that [187] may be the better able to serve {King Augustus}, since he does not like to trouble [181] so often in this. On [188] and the baronial diploma, he promises the enclosed letter and the draft he has corrected. [187] asks that once {Baron Schenck}'s diploma has passed it be returned, since for lack of time and people no copy could be kept. [The rest of f. 304v, on the tumults, submissions and an affirmative and negative answer, is read only in part.]

The happy relief of Barcelona is confirmed, but some circumstances vary: whether the Count of Toulouse was beaten at sea, and 2) whether the Duke of Anjou withdrew to Roussillon. The first and the last are made doubtful, [it being said] that Toulouse, by order of the French court, took himself to safety in the harbour of Toulon without hindrance from the English; but that the Duke of Anjou found no way open for his retreat and was so shut in that a retreat would be impossible for him. At the French court there is said therefore to be great dismay, especially since Mons. Berwick's army has mostly surrendered to Mylord Galloway […]. Mylord Galloway is said to have entered Madrid with great solemnity, everything acclaimed for King Charles on 24 May, and the Duke of Anjou to have fled from there altogether.

In Hungary the hope of peace [?grows], for next Monday His Grace the Bishop of Olmütz with Count Wratislaw, His Excellency, will go to Pressburg, where the congress is to be. [The rest, on a tumult at Vienna, Villars's weakened army on the Rhine after Marsin's march to Spain, a liberation the Emperor has at heart, General von Gross's retreat to Liegnitz, and [166]'s report not yet presented, is read only in part.]

Close: Monsieur, your very humble and very obedient Sidon. Vienna, 29 May 1706.
