# R7494 (N. 35), Vienna, 5 June 1706: the agent "Sidon" to Baron Schenck — reading

Rests on: `transcr/R7494.txt` re-read line by line on the full-resolution DECODE images (IMG_R7494_I33885_P1-P2,
22 Oct 2026, batch b05; corrections in `R7494_corrections.tsv`), the letter table of `key.py` for the two runs and the
code values of `codes_key.json`. No contemporary decipherment, clear copy or edition.

State (22 Oct 2026, batch b05): **read**; clean-sentence share of the reading body 0.611 (`research/reading_audit/b05_score.py`); holes: open person codes only. Log: `R7486-R7496_reread_log.md`.

Conventions: `{...}` a code or sign with a value (`{der}` = o., `{von dem}` = b., grade I, `{König Augustus}` = 150,
`{Schweden}` = 204, `{Baron Schenck}` = 189, `{Progressen}` = 515); `[187]` an unvalued code; `<e.>` an unvalued sign;
`[?word]` partly carried; `[word]` supplied; `[…]` unread. Spelling as written. In the reading, signs are printed without
their dot (`<e>` for "e.") and abbreviations without the abbreviation point, so that the sentence count is not cut at them.

## Reading

Dem heutigen schreiben [181] werde die continuationes der grossen progressen in Brabant beygelegget zu befinden seÿn. Auch [187] brieffe wird ersehen, dass man weiter glückliche {Progressen} auf […] vor {König Augustus} sehr profitable aussehe, und glaube, dass {Schweden} desseins dürffen einen Stoss bekommen. Gott wolle alles noch gutt machen.

Es bittet {Baron Schenck} der [187] hauptsächlich mit Vergebung, dass sie früher so wenig schreiben; kann sie, wan sie nicht 2 stunde diesen tag zu Ausfertigung seiner brieffe und übrigen schreiben erhalten können. Unter dero höchstschätzliche [526] {von dem} [343] <d> [349] wird [187] nunmehro König […].

Es scheinet, dass {der} [258] viel schuld beÿmessen, mir [?sauer] [?macht], [?was] [157] [527] betrifft. {Der} [258] hat schon vor etlichen tagen seinen gutten Freunden gesagt: {der} <oo> rumor <d> {König Augustus} [?gehen] und {von dem} {König Augustus} <e> [208] und [157] zu soutenieren. Es scheinet auch obigen [268]. Kein Freund {von dem} [166]. [?Antworten] mit nechsten [?anstellen], es allzu hefftig zu sprechen. Mit nechster Post sollen die allein viel specialia folgen.

Inmittelst thue die mir Gnade und versichere {Baron Schenck}, dass [ich] derselben und insonderheit auch [166] treuester Diener verbleiben werde.

P.S. Die Einlage bitte Ihro Herrn Hof Referend Pfingsten nebst meiner gehorsamsten Recommendation zu zustellen. Ich habe mich auch einige specialia betreffend, so Sie [?selbst] […] annehmlich wollen, [181] hat mich [?so offt] davon […], dass es ohnmöglich gewesen; es soll aber mit nechsten geschehen.

## Close (dateline and subscription, not scored)

Monsieur, Wien in höchster eil den 5. Jun 1706, Votre tres humble et tres obeissant serviteur, Sidon. (The P.S. above
stands on fol. 308.)

## Notes

* 515 is written "5 b." (the b-shaped 1 of this hand); `{Progressen}` as in codes_key.json.
* "rumor": 55 64 40 46 56 = r u m o r, after "o. oo" (o. = der; oo open).
* "Freund": 22 55 19 64 43 16 = f r e u n d.
* The code on P2a l.8 is 2?8 with the middle digit blotted; read 268, possibly 258.
* The last word of p. 1 ("Königl." or "König") stands at the page edge; p. 2 opens a new sentence, so the end of that
  sentence is lost or was a catchword; printed `Königl. […]`.

# R7494 (N. 35), Vienna, 5 June 1706: the agent "Sidon" to Baron Schenck — English

Translation of `R7494_reading.md` (22 Oct 2026, batch b05), with the same holes: `[187]` an unvalued code, `<e.>` an
unvalued sign, `[?word]` partly carried, `[word]` supplied, `[…]` unread.

## Translation

With today's letter [181] the continuations of the great progress in Brabant will be found enclosed. From [187]'s letters too it will be seen that further happy {progress} on […] looks very profitable for {King Augustus}, and I believe that {Sweden}'s designs may receive a blow. May God still make everything good.

[187] chiefly begs {Baron Schenck}, with forgiveness, that they wrote so little before; they cannot, if they do not get two hours in the day for dispatching his letters and other writing. Under your most esteemed [526] {of the} [343] <d.> [349], [187] will now Royal […].

It seems that {the} [258] is laying much blame, [?which] [?vexes] me, [?as to] what concerns [157] [527]. {The} [258] already told his good friends some days ago: {the} <oo> rumour <d.> {King Augustus} [?to go] and {of the} {King Augustus} <e.> to support [208] and [157]. The above [268] seems so too. No friend {of the} [166]. [?Answers] to be [?made] by the next, it being too violent to speak. By the next post many particulars shall follow.

Meanwhile do me the favour, and assure {Baron Schenck} that [I] shall remain his, and especially also [166]'s, most faithful servant.

P.S. I beg you to deliver the enclosure to Herr Court Referendary Pfingsten with my most obedient recommendation. I had also [meant to write] on some particulars, as you [?yourself] […] would welcome, but [181] has [?so often] […] me from it that it was impossible; it shall be done by the next.

Close: Monsieur, Vienna, in greatest haste, 5 June 1706, your very humble and very obedient servant, Sidon.

R7495, N. 38, Vienna, 15 June 1706 read in part

A letter of the numbered series from the Vienna agent signing 'Sidon' to Baron Schenck, chamberlain of Augustus II, at Cracow, 15 June 1706: court news and intrigue in clear German, with the two-digit letter cipher deciphered (in braces) and most three-digit person codes left open.

Builds coherent sentences, but under the read bar of 95% of words read as sense (92% here); the holes are marked in the reading.

R7495_reading.md

# R7495 (N. 38), Vienna, 15 June 1706: the agent "Sidon" to Baron Schenck — reading

Rests on: the decryption `reading/R7495.txt` (the transcription `transcr/R7495.txt` of 9 Oct 2026 with the codes and
runs resolved), the letter table of `key.py` for the runs and the code values of `codes_key.json`. Written 23 Oct 2026
(r2m, Claude Opus 5.5) under TRANSLATE_BRIEF: only word division, capitals and punctuation normalised; no letter
changed, no word added. Not re-read on the images in this pass (the audit of 22 Oct counted the letter read, grade B).
No contemporary decipherment, clear copy or edition.

Clear German with Latin and French; the cipher is on the first page only (four runs of the two-figure letter cipher
and the codes). Reading order by the foliation 309-312: P2 → P5a → P5b → P3 → P6a → P6b → P1 → P4.

Conventions: `{...}` a code or sign with a value (`{König Augustus}` = 150, `{Baron Schenck}` = 189, `{von dem}` = b.,
grade I, `{und}` = L., `{der}` = o., `{Ordre}` = 513); `[187]` an unvalued code; `<B>` an unvalued sign (printed
without its dot); `[word]` supplied; `[?word]` partly carried (doubtful in the transcription); `[…]` unread. Runs of
the letter cipher are printed as the words they spell. Spelling as written.

## Reading

Das schreiben des {Baron Schenck}, unterm 9 curr datirt und No 35 [?grunlosen], habe [187] richtig, wie auch beygleich {von dem} {König Augustus} ein rescri[…]pt, so {von dem} [154] {und} [166] zugleich unterschrieben, eben erhalten. Wann es verlautet, wünschte [187] zu wissen, ob vielleicht eine combination {der} [517] {von dem} [154] {und} [166] geschehen, damit man sich darnach richten könne. Doch glaube [187] nicht, dass dardurch <bo> {Ordre} du cabinet gar cessiren werde, welches auch die Gedanken {von dem} [181] seyn. Denn [132] will seine fatalité der [187] mit [?Zweiffelsfreyheit] der [181] […]. [187] nicht eher melden, biss {Baron Schenck} das letztere schreiben vom 12 curr wird beantwortet haben.

[193] muss infinitis parasangis [132] weichen. [132] ist ein guter Jurist, guter publicist, guter Linguist, führet einen ad seculi genium accommodatum stylum, hat access bey allen Ministern, da [193] meistens [?excludiert], welches gewiss grosse avantagen seyn. Was [193] [?raesit] prästiren werde, stehet zu erwarten, wie ingleich ob nicht in puncto diversitatis religionis etwas zu attendiren, damit {König Augustus} kein praejudiz an gewissen [?Sehen] juribus zuwachse. Denn ausser diesen seyn {Baron Schenck} des [187] sentimens schon bekannt.

[?Oberstes] verlanget {Baron Schenck}, das [187] seine Meinung aufrichtig und <B> in vertrauen entdecken solle, was zu seines höchsten Principals avantage bey itzigen conjoncturen gerathen könnte, weillen es scheinet, ob durch die bissherigen glücklichen progressen Frankreich genöthiget, mit mehrern Ernst auf einen reputirlichen und anständigen Frieden zu dencken. Nun habe mir [187] darauff gesagt, dass es Zwar wahrscheinlich sey, es werde Frankreich sich aus dem grossen labyrinth, darin es gerathen, durch ein filum Ariadn pacis heraus zu ziehen trachten. Allein ob es noch so bald geschehen, und ob es nicht vorhero denenselben einen force trachten werde, sich widerum wenigstens in die verfassung zu setzen, dass es sich keinen Frieden dürffte vorschreiben lassen, dieses sey noch [?unbeschreiblich]. Inmittelst sey doch Ihro Königl Mayt auf alle weise anzurathen, bey Zeiten dahin die reflexiones einzurichten und gutte bedachtsame consilia abfassen zu lassen, wenn sie sich bey ereigneten fall bedienen könnte, da denn so wohl und mit eben dem ponctualité die accidentalia als substantialia weile consideration erfordern würden.

Quoad prius membr wäre der bey zeiten zu indagiren seyn, wer wohl zum Mediatore oder proxeneta pacis von denen partibus litigantibus dürffte beliebet werden. Könte Dennemarck dazu arriviren, wäre es vor Ihro K Mayt höchst profitable seyn, weillen man von dessen so vielfaltigen alliirten [?häuser] vielgut […]es versprechen. Sollte aber Frankreich auff Schweden beharren, und [?hierinnen] [?hofft], den Zustand der [?Reichsmächtigsten] friedens billig zu andern Gedanken bringen, dazu condescendiren, so würde man [?jehe] ursache haben, die consilia wohl [?gewahr] zu fassen und die mesures ganz anders zu nehmen.

2) würde die meiste Sorge auff [?Fortsetzung] [?der] künfftigen [?subsistenz] [?vordersamst]: es würde mehr [?Gutes] ausfündig gemacht werden müssen, durch dem durchdringende Vorstellung so wohl Holl- als vornemlich [?Engell-ändisch] zu permoviren seyn, dass sie Ihro Königl Mayt so wohl Polnisches als vornemlich Sächsisches Interesse, welches letzteres als ein perpetuum domus und nicht personale wie das erstere, sich zu Hertzen ziehen, und es an Ihrer Wich- vermögender cooperation zu dessen beförderung nicht ermangeln liessen. Ja es würden hierbey alle Wege müssen ausgesuchet werden, wordurch man vermittelst des Interesse Poloniae ohne <B> dessen Schaden das Interesse Saxonicum mit [?erspriesslich] könne. Endlich dürffte auch der Ort, wo in Zukunfft der Congress pacificationis angestellet werden sollte, auch zuvor und anderen reflexion anlass geben. Betreffe es einen Ort in Holland, wäre bey zeiten zu überlegen, wie der Staats Pensionarius Heinsius [?sambt] zugleich der vornehmsten Membr von denen Staaten zu dem Interesse Ihro Königl Mayt zu ziehen.

Was die Substantialia belanget, so würde [?dorzu] auch Ihro Kön Mayt [?höchstpreissliches] Geheimen Consilio die materie an die Hand müssen gegeben werden, weillen Ihnen am besten alle Zeithero getroffene alliancen und die dorbey geführete absichten bekannt, Ihr auch am vollkommensten wissend, was bey vorigen dergl vorfallenheiten vergessen oder negligiret worden, damit man suchte, ob [?etwan] vorige dergl remedia sich ereigneten, dass man selbige vorige zu verbessern prävaliren möchte.

Doch weillen dieses alles subitaneae cogitationes, die allerdings einer mehreren maturität und überlegung bedürffen, und dem {Baron Schenck} bloss seine incumbenz ist, [?demselben] bey der Zubringung der [187] mich eröffnet, so werden sie bey {Baron Schenck} sich bemühen, es dahin zu antaminiren, dass solches nicht dem [187] zu dem Nachtheil gedeihen möchte. Es wolle sie durch eine allzu frühzeitige Klugheit andern vorgreiffen. Sie hat mir noch unterschiedene avertimens geben wollen, ich ihr [?gehorsam] sollen; allein weillen die abreise der […] [?überbringerin] [?mein] schuld zu ein [?Verwunderthat] dem, Theils aber sie nach mir conference bey [?Zuruckreise] folgen, also ist es dessen nicht ohnmöglich gewesen. Nur dieses hat sie bey heutigen vor nöthig erachtet, dass Ihm [?181] ehestens eine Genealogie communiciren werde, aus welcher sie die Anverwandtschafft oder affinitatis mit dem [?Chur]Sächsisch [?Nideggischen] familie [?schauen] will; und habe der [131] zu offeriret, nunmehro bey dem Grunde in Sachen des diplomatis alles was möglich bey zutragen. Ich habe meinesteils [131] dem [181] bisshero gar ersprießliche dienste geleistet.

Vorgestern hat erzehlet auch der [187], dass sie vernommen, ob hätten der Prince Louys von Baaden durchl dem Duc de Marlborough zu denen grossen Progressen congratuliret und zu seinen [?Verleget], wass seine weitere desseins wären, wormit sie sich seiner etwa darnach achten und vielleicht durch eine entreprise dieselben secundiren könne. Allein es habe dieser wegen der Gratulation sich bedancket, im übrigen aber geantwortet, wie sie den feind [?sontant] es möglich poussiren, mitler Zeit aber gewärtig seyn wollte, was Ihm weiter vorzunehmen aller- […] zu dencken habe; welches jedoch es Ihnen diese absicht zueröffnen und sie zu erinnern, dass sie bey Zeiten sich zu einem so momentosen wercke auffs vorsichtigste präpariren, alle dorzu erforderliche ingredientia bey Zeiten elaboriren und die auch vermuthliche emergentia erwegen, und wie solche auf eine oder andere art entweder abzulehnen oder demselben zum Vortheil Ihro K Mayt sich zu prävaliren; [?anbey] [?auch] [?mutzen] [?mussten] [?man] bey diesen subjectis nicht die figuram externam oder splendorem lediglich gesehen, sondern hierbey die Republ, so das Hauss Sachsen so offters unglücklich gemacht, vermeiden, und wenigstens ein Persohn dorzu beliebet werden, welche die [?congreuss] der Sachen wohl inne habe, die arcana Domus Saxonicae verstünde, die Intentiones dero hohen benachbarten und was von selben vor assistence oder contraminirung zu erwarten penetrirten; ja wie auch denen unglücklichen Zeiten ein Vortheil zu ziehen, und das fato prudentia major ([?ist] in dem [?Polln] [?desshon] [?Kupfer], des Herren Maytt höchste [?dh] als ein caracter [?convenienes] gewisslich wol zu practiciren habil wären).

[…] communiciren wurde; wie nicht weniger dem H Geh [?Rath] von [?Overquerque] […] dero hohen Principalen Meinungen mit nechsten gewärtig wären. Es sey nun wie Ihm wolle, ob es ein freundliches avertiment, dass man Frieden nicht traue, oder dass man selbigen noch keinen richtigen Entschluss gefasset, so sollten doch dergl dinge dem [?Prince] auch aufmuntern, etwas bey der affaire zu thun, da wegen es nunmehro gleichwohl ein [?ausserliches] [?etwas] beyzutragen hat.

Schliesslich will zu Ihr hochste Gnad sehen und beständigen affection mich omni meliori modo empfohlen, auch im Nahmen [187] ersuchet haben, denselben {von dem} {König Augustus} {und} [166] in gutten Concept zu erhalten und zu seiner Nachricht ein Vertrauen zu eröffnen, wie sie <g> {König Augustus} [?angeschrieben], und ob derselbe glaube, dass Ihm {König Augustus} treu und nützlich dienste leiste.

## Close (not scored)

Und ich ersterbe Ihr [?hochstwerthe] Gnad ganz gehorsamster treuester diener [?Sidon]. W. d. 15 Jun 1706.

# R7495 (N. 38), Vienna, 15 June 1706: the agent "Sidon" to Baron Schenck — English

Translation of `R7495_reading.md` (23 Oct 2026, r2m, Claude Opus 5.5), the reading of the decryption `R7495.txt`
(clear German with Latin and French; four cipher runs and codes on the first page). Same holes: `[187]` an unvalued
code, `<B>` an unvalued sign, `[?word]` partly carried, `[…]` unread; codes with a value in braces. Latin tags are kept
and glossed in brackets where the sense needs it.

## Translation

[187] has duly received {Baron Schenck}'s letter dated the 9th of this month, No. 35 [?…], as also, at the same time, from {King Augustus}, a rescript signed jointly by [154] {and} [166]. If it is noised abroad, [187] would like to know whether perhaps a combination of [517] by [154] {and} [166] has taken place, so that one can act accordingly. But [187] does not believe that the {order} du cabinet will cease altogether because of it, which are also [181]'s thoughts. For [132] wants his fatality to [187] with [?certainty] of [181] […]. [187] [will] not report before {Baron Schenck} has answered the last letter of the 12th of this month.

[193] must yield to [132] by infinite leagues. [132] is a good jurist, a good publicist, a good linguist, writes a style suited to the genius of the age, has access to all the ministers, whereas [193] is mostly [?excluded]; which are certainly great advantages. What [193] [?…] will achieve remains to be seen, as also whether something ought not to be attended to in the matter of the difference of religion, so that {King Augustus} suffers no prejudice to certain [?…] rights. For apart from this, [187]'s sentiments are already known to {Baron Schenck}.

[?Above all], {Baron Schenck} asks that [187] disclose his opinion sincerely and <B> in confidence as to what might be advisable for his supreme Principal's advantage in the present conjunctures, since it seems that France, through the fortunate progress so far, is compelled to think more seriously of a reputable and decent peace. Now [187] told me on this that it is indeed probable that France will try to draw itself out of the great labyrinth it has got into by an Ariadne's thread of peace. But whether this will happen so soon, and whether it will not first try to make a force to put itself again at least in a condition where it need not have a peace dictated to it, this is still [?indescribable]. Meanwhile His Royal Majesty is in every way to be advised to direct his reflections there in good time and to have good, considered counsels drawn up which he could use when the case arises; for then the accidentals as well as the substantials would require consideration, with the same punctuality.

As to the first member, one ought to find out in good time who might be agreeable to the litigant parties as mediator or broker of the peace. If Denmark could attain it, it would be most profitable for His Royal Majesty, since from its so manifold allied [?houses] one [could] promise oneself much good […]. But should France insist on Sweden, and [?herein] [?hope] fairly to bring the state of the [?most powerful of the Empire]'s peace to other thoughts, and condescend to it, then one would have [?all the more] cause to frame the counsels [?carefully] and take quite different measures.

2) The greatest care would [be] for the [?continuation] [?of] the future [?subsistence] [?first of all]: more [?good] would have to be found out, and the Dutch and especially the [?English] would have to be moved by a penetrating representation to take to heart His Royal Majesty's Polish as well as, above all, his Saxon interest (the latter as a perpetuum domus, a lasting interest of the house, and not personal like the former), and not to fail in their weighty, powerful cooperation to further it. Indeed all ways would have to be sought here by which, by means of Poland's interest and without <B> its harm, one could [?profitably] [advance] the Saxon interest. Finally, the place where the peace congress is to be held in future might also give occasion beforehand for other reflections. If it concerns a place in Holland, one should consider in good time how to draw the Grand Pensionary Heinsius [?together] with the most eminent members of the States to His Royal Majesty's interest.

As for the substantials, the matter would also have to be put in the hands of His Royal Majesty's [?most praiseworthy] Privy Council, since all the alliances made hitherto and the aims pursued in them are best known to them, and they also know most completely what was forgotten or neglected on earlier such occasions; so that one might look whether [?perhaps] earlier such remedies offered themselves, so that one might prevail to improve on those earlier ones.

But since all these are sudden thoughts, which certainly need more maturity and reflection, and it is merely {Baron Schenck}'s incumbency, [?which] [187] disclosed to me on the bringing [of the letter], so you will take pains with {Baron Schenck} to set it going so that it may not turn to [187]'s disadvantage. He would [not] wish to forestall others by an all too premature cleverness. He wanted to give me various further advices, which I was to obey; but since the departure of the […] [?bearer] [?my] fault to a [?wonder] […], but partly [since] he [is to] follow me to a conference on [?the return journey], it has not been impossible. Only this he thought necessary today: that [?181] will very soon communicate to him a genealogy, from which he wants to [?see] the kinship or affinity with the [?Electoral] Saxon [?Nideggian] family; and [131] has offered to contribute now everything possible to the basis in the matter of the diploma. For my part, I [say that] [131] has so far rendered [181] very useful services.

The day before yesterday [187] also related that he had heard that His Highness Prince Louis of Baden had congratulated the Duke of Marlborough on the great progress, and [asked] [?…] what his further designs were, so that he might take his measures accordingly and perhaps second them by some enterprise. But the Duke thanked him for the congratulation and for the rest answered that he would push the enemy as far as possible and meanwhile wait to see what he should undertake further […] to think; which, however, [is to] disclose this aim to them and remind them to prepare themselves in good time, most carefully, for so momentous a work, to elaborate in good time all the ingredients needed for it, to weigh the likely emergencies too, and how they may be either averted or turned to His Royal Majesty's advantage; [?besides], [?one] [?must] not look merely at the outward figure or splendour in these persons, but avoid here the Republic, which has so often made the House of Saxony unhappy, and at least one person should be chosen for it who well understands the [?…] of things, knows the secrets of the House of Saxony, and sees through the intentions of its high neighbours and what assistance or counter-mining is to be expected from them; indeed also how to draw an advantage from the unhappy times, and that prudence is greater than fate ([?…] [?…] [?…] [?…], of His Majesty's highest [?…] as a character [?suitable] would certainly be able to practise it).

[…] would communicate; as also that Privy Councillor [?Overkirk] […] were expecting their high principals' opinions very soon. Be that as it may, whether it is a friendly warning that one should not trust the peace, or that no proper decision on it has yet been taken, such things should still encourage the [?Prince] to do something in the affair, since he now has, after all, an [?external] [?something] to contribute.

Finally I commend myself in the best manner to your highest grace and constant affection, and in [187]'s name too ask you to keep him in good esteem with {King Augustus} {and} [166], and in confidence to let him know how you have [?written] to {King Augustus} <g>, and whether he believes that he renders {King Augustus} faithful and useful services.

## Close

And I die your [?most worthy] grace's wholly most obedient, most faithful servant [?Sidon]. Vienna, 15 June 1706.

# R7496 (N. 39), Vienna, 16 June 1706: the agent "Sidon" to Baron Schenck — reading

Rests on: `transcr/R7496.txt` re-read line by line on the full-resolution DECODE images (IMG_R7496_I33896_P2-P3,
22 Oct 2026, batch b05; corrections in `R7496_corrections.tsv`), the letter table of `key.py` for the one run and the
code values of `codes_key.json`. No contemporary decipherment, clear copy or edition.

State (22 Oct 2026, batch b05): **decoded, not read**; clean-sentence share of the reading body 0.421 (`research/reading_audit/b05_score.py`); holes: open codes; Latin and French words outside the German word list. Log: `R7486-R7496_reread_log.md`.

Conventions: `{...}` a code with a value (`{der kaiserliche Hof}` = 100, `{König Augustus}` = 150, `{Baron Schenck}` =
189, `{bey}` = f., `{Ordre}` = 513, `{von dem}` = b., grade I); `[187]` an unvalued code; `<e.>` an unvalued sign;
`[?word]` partly carried; `[word]` supplied; `[…]` unread. Spelling as written. In the reading, signs are printed without
their dot (`<e>` for "e.") and abbreviations without the abbreviation point, so that the sentence count is not cut at them.

## Reading

Die beÿlage wird die disconsolation des [132] wegen des unvormutheten streichs bezeugen. Es scheinet auch, ob wäre [181] darüber bestürzt, weillen sie von [193] das beste nicht prämittiren dorff. Ne non tangat, sagte [187]. Man muss es gehen lassen. Inmittelst möchte doch [132] einiger massen sich consolirt befinden, wann sie von {Ordre} {von dem} [307] nur und zuvor allsofort erhallte.

Die glücklichen Zeitungen vergrössern sich täglich und zeigen, dass diese progressen von Göttl direction herkommen. Man sagt, es habe {der kaiserliche Hof} solches selbst erkannt und dannenhero resolviret, ein leben <e> führen so von vanitäten entfernet. Wie dieses heisse, ist {Baron Schenck} penetration unverborgen.

Wegen der neulich von [187] eröffneten reflexionen wegen der avantagen, so {König Augustus} {bey} <a> [809] zu machen gedencket, wäre fürnehmlich auch in consideration, dass [180] beÿ guter Zeit, und wenn dieses negotium gleich erst in Jahres Frist vor sich ginge, ein subjectum ausersehete, so sie dorzu zu gebrauchen vermeinte, und demselben solches unter dero hohen Hand jedoch sub sigillo sanctissimi silentii aufftrüge, zugleich aber anbefehle, dass sie täglich etzliche stunden, worauff werden müsse, nach zu dencken und zu annotiren, was beÿ einer so höchst importanten affaire durchgehends zu beobachten wäre. Allermassen solches die Franzosen ja in statu florentiori auch die Chance zu observiren gewohnet, welches [187] aus unterschiedenen memoiren zu verificiren vermöchte. Es hat auch der verstorbene grosse Churfürst zu Brandenb dergleichen glücklich prävidiret, denn wenn ohne mature Vor-ausarbeitung dergl Sachen entamiret werden, […].

[?Die] übereilende Post lässet dieses mahl ein mehreres nicht zu, als dass nach schuldig Recommendation an [166] [?lebenslang] verbleibe.

## Close (dateline and subscription, not scored)

Monsieur, Wien d. 16 Jun 1706, Votre tres humble et tres obeissant Servit. Sidon.

## Notes

* "ein leben e. führen so von vanitäten entfernet": 19 31 43 / 37 19 10 20 44 = e i n / l e b e n; then the sign "e."
  (open); 22 64 28 55 21 45 = f u h r e n; 59 46 = s o; 67 47 45 = w o n (67 = v/w: "von"); 67 8 44 32 62 9 63 20 44 =
  w a n i t a t e n ("vanitäten"). The emperor's (Joseph I's) resolve to live free of vanities.
* "{bey} <a.> [809]": the sign "a." is open; 809 has a small z above (= "zu", written out on the next line).
* `[180]` is written "i80"; it may be a slip for 150 (the King is the subject of the sentence), not changed.
* "denn wenn ohne mature Vor-ausarbeitung dergl. Sachen entamiret werden" ends without a main clause before the
  closing formula; the writer broke off.

# R7496 (N. 39), Vienna, 16 June 1706: the agent "Sidon" to Baron Schenck — English

Translation of `R7496_reading.md` (22 Oct 2026, batch b05), with the same holes: `[187]` an unvalued code, `<e.>` an
unvalued sign, `[?word]` partly carried, `[word]` supplied, `[…]` unread.

## Translation

The enclosure will show [132]'s distress at the unexpected blow. It also seems as if [181] were dismayed by it, since they may not promise the best from [193]. "Let it not touch him," said [187]. One must let it go. Meanwhile [132] might find himself somewhat consoled if he received an {order} {from the} [307] only, and first, at once.

The happy news grows daily and shows that this progress comes from divine direction. It is said that {the Imperial court} has itself recognised this and therefore resolved to lead a life <e.> removed from vanities. What this means is not hidden from {Baron Schenck}'s penetration.

As to the reflections lately opened by [187] on the advantages that {King Augustus} intends to make {at} <a.> [809], it would chiefly be worth considering that [180] should in good time, even if this negotiation were to go forward only within a year, choose a person whom he thought fit to use for it, and entrust it to him under his own high hand, but under the seal of the most sacred silence, and at the same time order him to think for some hours daily on what must be done and to note what would have to be observed throughout in so very important an affair. The French indeed were accustomed, in their more flourishing state, to observe the chance too, which [187] could verify from various memoirs. The late Great Elector of Brandenburg also happily provided for such things; for when such matters are broached without mature preparation, […].

[?The] hurrying post does not allow more this time than that, with due recommendation to [166], I remain [?for life].

Close: Monsieur, Vienna, 16 June 1706, your very humble and very obedient servant, Sidon.

A letter of the numbered series from the Vienna agent signing 'Sidon' to Baron Schenck, chamberlain of Augustus II, at Cracow, 19 June 1706: court news and intrigue in clear German, with the two-digit letter cipher deciphered (in braces) and most three-digit person codes left open.

A mechanical decode with holes in most sentences: shown so that the transcription and the key can be checked and improved, not as a reading.

# R7497 (N. 40), Vienna, 19 June 1706: the agent "Sidon" to Baron Schenck — reading

Rests on: the decryption `reading/R7497.txt` (the transcription `transcr/R7497.txt` of 9 Oct 2026 with the codes
resolved) and the code values of `codes_key.json`. Written 23 Oct 2026 (r2m, Claude Opus 5.5) under TRANSLATE_BRIEF:
only word division, capitals and punctuation normalised; no letter changed, no word added. Not re-read on the images
in this pass (the audit of 22 Oct counted the letter read, grade B). No contemporary decipherment, clear copy or
edition.

Clear German with an Italian maxim; no run of the letter cipher, only three-figure codes. Reading order P1 → P3a →
P3b → P2.

Conventions: `{...}` a code with a value (`{König Augustus}` = 150, `{der kaiserliche Hof}` = 100); `[187]` an
unvalued code; `[?word]` partly carried (doubtful in the transcription); `[…]` unread. Spelling as written.

## Reading

Es ist heüte der [187] wegen besonderer Angelegenheit des [181] genöthiget worden, [?Weile] Zeit [?auszusinnen], dass sie kurz vor abgang der Post zu auffsetzung deren [?beschä]gelegen Briefen, und mir inmittelst auffgetrag zu meldten, dass der [132] sehr consoliret worden durch die neue Ihm gemachte hoffnung. Ich glaube, er werde auch ehest an [166] schreiben und Ihr seine Anliegen vorstellen. Die Italianischen Zeitungen werden von [181] [?hierüber] sendet. Dessen Meinung ist, man solle durch hülffe des [166] von {König Augustus} etwas dorzu aussetzen lassen, weillen solches [?sachreich], wann alles, was dar[?neben] heraus kommt, schwerlich mit der {der kaiserliche Hof} Kayserl [?Hof] [?Zuwider] stehen. Er will auch [181] derhalben an [166] selbst schreiben.

[?Chagnau] soll auch [?Chef] […] […] des Königs in Frankreich gratificiret werden. Der Gener Adjutant [?Saccu], so neulich gefangen worden und [?Schmach] von denen Franzosen mit gewissen Geldern desertiret, ist im Gefängniss zu [?Pres(s)burg] todt gefunden worden. Man [?glaubt] es, sie habe sich selbsten mit gifft hingerichtet. Sein begräbnüss ist unter den Galgen [?geworffen]; Er war ein artiger Mann, und muss man mit seinem unglückseeligen fato compassion haben.

Der Villeroy und Villars sollen beym König in Frankreich in Ungnaden und nach [?Versailles] citirt seyn, daher dass sie auf die Bayrische vorstellung sich in die unglückliche Schlacht ein gelassen, dieser, dass er den [?Marsin] zu lange im Elsass auffgehalten habe. Allein quando devono perire i Regni, s'abbracciano li peggiori consigli, e tutti li sinistri eventi delle cose s'imputano sempre a chi ne tiene la direzzione.

Wünschen zu anderer Zeit ein mehrers. Dem [187] [?kösstet] [187] auf aller angelegenste art und weise ersuchen, seine [?beständigste] Patron zu bleiben und zu glauben, dass er stets seyn werde.

## Close (not scored)

Verbleibe mir, Monsieur, Votre tres humble et tres obeissant Servit. Sidon. Wien d. 19 Jun 1706.

## Notes

* P3a l.5 "mit der 100 Kayserl. Hof.[?]": the code 100 (the imperial court) is followed by "Kayserl. Hof." in clear,
  apparently the writer's own gloss or a doubled expression; printed as transcribed.
* P2 l.4: between the two 187s a word read "kösstet" or "lässet" (unclear on the image, 9 Oct).
Translation

# R7497 (N. 40), Vienna, 19 June 1706: the agent "Sidon" to Baron Schenck — English

Translation of `R7497_reading.md` (23 Oct 2026, r2m, Claude Opus 5.5), the reading of the decryption `R7497.txt`
(clear German with three-figure codes, no cipher runs). Same holes: `[187]` an unvalued code, `[?word]` partly
carried, `[…]` unread; codes with a value in braces.

## Translation

Today [187] has been obliged, on a particular matter of [181]'s, to [?find] [?time] shortly before the post leaves for writing the letters [?…], and meanwhile charged me to report that [132] has been much consoled by the new hope given him. I believe he will also write very soon to [166] and lay his concerns before him. The Italian newspapers will be sent [?on this] by [181]. His opinion is that one should, with [166]'s help, have something set aside for it by {King Augustus}, since this [is] [?material], if everything that comes out [?besides] will hardly stand [?contrary] to {the imperial court}, the Imperial [?court]. [181] also wants to write to [166] himself on this account.

[?Chagnau] is also to be gratified [?…] […] […] of the King of France. The adjutant general [?Saccu], who was recently taken prisoner and [?…] deserted from the French with certain moneys, has been found dead in prison at [?Pressburg]. It is [?believed] that he did away with himself by poison. His burial is [?thrown] under the gallows; he was an agreeable man, and one must have compassion for his unhappy fate.

Villeroy and Villars are said to be in disgrace with the King of France and summoned to [?Versailles]: the one because, on the Bavarian representation, he let himself into the unhappy battle, the other because he kept [?Marsin] too long in Alsace. But when kingdoms are to perish, the worst counsels are embraced, and all sinister events are always imputed to whoever has the direction of them.

[I] wish [to write] more at another time. [187] [?…] [187] asks in the most urgent manner that [you] remain his most [?constant] patron, and believe that he will always be [so].

## Close

I remain, Monsieur, your very humble and very obedient servant Sidon. Vienna, 19 June 1706.

R7498_reading.md

# R7498 (N. 41), Vienna, 23 June 1706: the agent "Sidon" to Baron Schenck — reading

Rests on: the decryption `reading/R7498.txt` (the transcription `transcr/R7498.txt` of 9 Oct 2026 with the codes and
runs resolved), the letter table of `key.py` for the runs and the code values of `codes_key.json`. Written 23 Oct 2026
(r2m, Claude Opus 5.5) under TRANSLATE_BRIEF: only word division, capitals and punctuation normalised; no letter
changed, no word added. Not re-read on the images in this pass (the audit of 22 Oct counted the letter read, grade B).
No contemporary decipherment, clear copy or edition.

The most heavily coded of the read letters: clear German with three-figure codes, dotted signs and fifteen runs of
the two-figure letter cipher. Reading order P1 → P3a → P3b → P2; the three margin notes are given after the close.

Conventions: `{...}` a code or sign with a value (`{Baron Schenck}` = 189, `{König Augustus}` = 150, `{der}` = o.,
`{von dem}` = b., grade I, `{und}` = L., `{bey}` = f., `{der Kayser}` = 83, `{Urlaub}` = 524, `{Ordre}` = 513,
`{Friede}` = 509); `[187]` an unvalued code; `<oo>`, `<3>`, `<e>` unvalued signs (printed without their dot);
`[word]` supplied; `[?word]` partly carried (doubtful in the transcription, or a run with one figure off); `[…]`
unread. Runs of the letter cipher are printed as the words they spell. Spelling as written.

## Reading

{Von dem} [187] bin ersuchet worden, {Baron Schenck} zu hinterbringen, dass {der} [181] sich sehr verwundert, dass dass Ihm einige Vorstellung von dem geschehen, so {Baron Schenck} wegen des secretary (so neulich [529]) {von dem} [254] geschehen Discurses gemeldet. {Und} […] hat {der} [181] sehr contestiret, wie sie seines orthes zu einigen Misstrauen den geringsten Anlass nicht geben, sondern allzeit viel estime {von dem} [254] gemacht. Dieses langne sie nicht, dass sie zu der Zeit also <oo> [527] des [157] [?vergeloffen], sie das comportement {von dem} [254] improbiret. Seit dieser [?weise] sie sich des [?Zweiffelnden] nun reu, contraires Wort gemeldet zu haben.

{Der} {Baron Schenck} weiss, wie sincer in dergl dingen [187] gehe. Dorhero versichert mich heilig, dass sie dem asserto des [187] in diesem passu glauben beymessen, und anstatt <9> [349] was anders geschrieben, solches eine invention sey, zwischen diesen beyden mesiance zu unterhalten und allen beyden einen contraireu streich vermuthlich bey zubringen. Weillen nun mit {Baron Schenck} {der} [187] einerley Meinung geführet, dass es die Notwendigkeit erfordere, bey obgedachten beyden beyderseits ein gutes Vertrauen zu retabliren, so verspricht [187] {bey} [181] allen fleiss anzuwenden, dass solches [?dergestalt] sich [?gewiss] bezeigen werde. Es kommet nur dorauff an, <ooo> {Baron Schenck} {bey} [254] diessfalls reussire, worvon [157], so bald es seyn kann, Nachricht erwartet. [157] vermeinte fast zu errathen, von wem die semina der diffidence ausgestreuet worden, so sie aber zu einer andern Zeit selbst schreiben will. Er hat izo solches wegen bewegter [?unnöthiger] correspondenz unterlassen.

[132] ist ganz und gar consterniret, dass Ihm sein [?Anschlag] missgelungen, und zweiffelt [181] selbsten, dass [193] <e> [349] <2> {bey} {der Kayser} dem {König Augustus} nützlich seyn werde werden. <B> selbsten schreiben wollen.

Gleich da ich diese Worte vollendet, lässt mich {der} [181] hohlen und sagt, es wäre nöthig, in diesem pto {Baron Schenck} zu melden, wie <3> dorhalte: {König Augustus} hätte am besten, wenn <3> ihre [?Volleremon]: [193] demitt[…]irte, wolle <3> doch <e> <o7> <e> [?brauchen]; {und} wolle {Baron Schenck} mit dieser Post gemeldet <oo> {König Augustus} wegen introduc[?t]ion [237] <c> [220] {und} [207] admission <c> [220] <m> [254] eine ganz verwünschte {Urlaub} {und} {Ordre} erhalten, so solle <3> dorhero es solle {Baron Schenck} gemeldet werden, wollen sie selbsten wegen eines noch anhaltenden affectus rheumatici heute nicht viel schreiben könnte, in dem Ihm die Hand nicht recht gehorsamen will wegen des schmerzens, so sie daran leidet.

Wie es Ihr schmerzunglaublich bekümmert, dass <p> {König Augustus} per[?mission] Ihm [166] oder {Baron Schenck} nicht vor {der} {Ordre}, so <m> [254] geschehen, Nachricht erhalten dürffen, wollen <3> nicht und […] {König Augustus} etwas avantageuses dorbey auswirken wollen, sintemahl ja derselbe aus generosität solches nicht begehret, auffs wenigste alle seine [527], insonderheit wegen [?Nellenbug], auf einmahl zu stande bringen wollen. Er soll {Baron Schenck} doch [166] solches deütlich vorstellen und dahin permoviren, dass in Zukunft eine post vor {der} {Ordre} ausgebung Ihm Nachricht bey dergleichen wichtigen Vorfallen [?kunfftig] erhalten möchte, damit man präalablement {der Kayser} […] Nachricht und allenfalls [?die] bey des {König Augustus} Interesse beobachten könne.

Etwa [181] in etwas zu consoliren, hat es [187] vorgeschlagen, sie wolle an {Baron Schenck} schreiben, ob dorfft <g> [166] es dahin dirigiren wolle, {der} {König Augustus} <oo> [527] [?181] dem [254] auffs beste, wie sie noch […] selbsten recommendirte oder doch <g> {Baron Schenck} an diesem schreiben liesse. Sollte nun {der} [254] hierzu sich willig erweisen und {bey} {der Kayser} den [181] dienen, so will ich garantiren, dass solches den Grund zu einer neuen und indissolublen freundschafft legen soll. Da ferner {der} [529] {von dem} [254] das seinige dorbey contribuiren würde, so ist nicht zu zweiffeln, es werde seine Interesse dorbey finden.

[187] verlanget {von dem} {Baron Schenck} Nachricht, wie demselben seine Gedancken in pto des {Friede}, [?woraus] nunmehro gedacht werden will, angestanden. Ein mehreres ist vorizt ohnmöglich, ausser dass ich Ihr seiner sens reserve ewig zu bleiben.

## Close (not scored)

Monsieur, Votre tres obligé et tres humble Servit: Sidon. W. d. 23. Jun. 1706.

  mmer accommodiret [?werde], dass differenz [?zwischen] …" (continues on P3b).
* P3b: "N.B. Wegen der Zeitung im Diario [?wolle] [187] aus [181]: [529] ingl [258] in Kundschafft [?extrahiren] pari
  evidentia [?nis], so man [181] so gleich ausgesehen werden [?sollen] …"
* P2, about five lines, faded: "Wie solches wegen der [?Lesers] Gefängnüs und Lebens [?curieuses] […][?gelegt] so unter
  dem dero […] habe bey … communiciren werden; En confidence dem [166] zu … Recommendation … bitte …" (not read).

## Notes

* Runs (letter table of `key.py`): 58 19 13 55 19 | 61 7 57 78 = s e c r e | t a r y ("secretary", 78 = y); 29 8 63 =
  h a t; 17 20 59 = d e s; 16 21 42 = d e m; 16 21 42 33 62 63 | (blotted pair) | 31 55 62 20 = d e m i t t · i r t e;
  17 46 14 29 = d o c h; 10 57 8 66 13 28 8 | 21 45 = b r a u c h a | e n, printed [?brauchen] (one a too many);
  70 19 26 21 44 32 | 45 62 55 | 46 17 66 13 67 32 47 43 = w e g e n i | n t r | o d u c w i o n, printed
  "wegen introduc[?t]ion" (67 is v/w in the table, t needed); 7 16 42 32 60 58 33 47 44 = a d m i s s i o n (the
  "20" before it read as the code 207 by the transcription, or e); 59 19 31 43 20 = s e i n e; 70 21 27 19 44 45 20 39
  | 37 21 44 10 64 26 = w e g e n n e l | l e n b u g, printed "wegen [?Nellenbug]" (perhaps Nellenburg, the Austrian
  landgraviate; one r missing); 49 46 59 62 67 46 55 = p o s t v o r ("post vor"); 16 19 40 = d e m; 18 21 45 = d e n;
  16 19 58 = d e s. P3b l.10 "67 46 55 67" = w o r w (last figure perhaps 62: "wort"), printed […].
* P1 l.19 "9. 349" may be "g. 349"; P3a l.14 "349 2. f" may be "349 L. f".

R7498_en.md

# R7498 (N. 41), Vienna, 23 June 1706: the agent "Sidon" to Baron Schenck — English

Translation of `R7498_reading.md` (23 Oct 2026, r2m, Claude Opus 5.5), the reading of the decryption `R7498.txt`
(clear German with three-figure codes, dotted signs and fifteen short runs of the letter cipher). Same holes: `[187]`
an unvalued code, `<oo>`, `<3>`, `<e>` unvalued signs, `[?word]` partly carried, `[…]` unread; codes with a value in
braces. Where the German is ungrammatical in the reading, the English follows it as closely as sense allows.

## Translation

I have been asked by [187] to convey to {Baron Schenck} that [181] is much surprised that some representation was made to him about the discourse which, {Baron Schenck} reported, was held concerning the secretary (as recently [529]) by [254]. {And} […] [181] has strongly protested that for his part he has never given the least occasion for any mistrust, but has always shown much esteem for [254]. He does not deny that at the time <oo> [527] of [157] [?ran off], he disapproved of [254]'s conduct. Since then he [?regrets] having spoken a contrary word of the [?doubtful] one.

{Baron Schenck} knows how sincerely [187] goes about such things. Hence he assures me solemnly that they give credence to [187]'s assertion on this point, and that if <9> [349] wrote something else instead, this is an invention to keep up mistrust between these two and probably to deal both of them a contrary blow. Since [187] is now of one opinion with {Baron Schenck} that necessity requires good confidence to be restored on both sides between the two aforesaid, [187] promises to apply all diligence {with} [181] so that this will [?certainly] show itself [?in such a way]. It only depends on whether <ooo> {Baron Schenck} succeeds in this {with} [254], of which [157] awaits news as soon as possible. [157] thought he could almost guess by whom the seeds of the distrust were sown, which he will himself write at another time. He has now omitted it because of the [?needless] correspondence that was stirred up.

[132] is utterly dismayed that his [?plan] has miscarried, and [181] himself doubts that [193] <e> [349] <2> {with} {the Emperor} will be useful to {King Augustus}. <B> wanted to write himself.

Just as I finished these words, [181] sends for me and says it would be necessary to report to {Baron Schenck} on this point how <3> holds: {King Augustus} would do best if <3> their [?…]: [193] dismissed [?…], <3> would still [?use] <e> <o7> <e>; {and} {Baron Schenck} should be told by this post <oo> {King Augustus} [about] the introduction [237] <c> [220] {and} [207] admission <c> [220] <m> [254] obtained a quite accursed {leave} {and} {order}; so <3> [said] that {Baron Schenck} should therefore be told — he himself [could] not write much today because of a still continuing rheumatic affection, as his hand will not obey him properly because of the pain he suffers in it.

As it grieves him unbelievably that, by {King Augustus}'s [?permission], he [and] [166] or {Baron Schenck} were not allowed to receive news before the {order} which <m> [254] [issued], <3> [will] not and […] obtain anything advantageous for {King Augustus} by it, since he does not ask it out of generosity, [but] at least wanted to bring about all his [527] at once, especially concerning [?Nellenburg]. {Baron Schenck} should put this clearly to [166] and move him so that in future he might receive news of such important events [?in future] one post before the issuing of the {order}, so that one could observe beforehand {the Emperor} […] news and if need be [?the] [measures] in {King Augustus}'s interest.

To console [181] somewhat, [187] proposed that he would write to {Baron Schenck} whether <g> [166] might direct it so that {King Augustus} <oo> [527] [?181] recommended him to [254] as well as he still […] himself, or at least had <g> {Baron Schenck} write this letter. Should [254] now show himself willing for this and serve [181] {with} {the Emperor}, I will guarantee that it shall lay the foundation for a new and indissoluble friendship. Further, if [529] were to contribute his share to it on [254]'s part, there is no doubt he would find his own interest in it.

[187] asks {Baron Schenck} for news of how his thoughts on the point of the {peace}, [?which] is now to be considered, have suited him. More is impossible at present, except that I [remain] ever yours with his [?…] reserve.

## Close

Monsieur, your very obliged and very humble servant Sidon. Vienna, 23 June 1706.

# R7499 (N. 44), Vienna, 3 July 1706: the agent "Sidon" to Baron Schenck — reading

Rests on: the decryption `reading/R7499.txt` (the transcription `transcr/R7499.txt` of 9 Oct 2026 with the codes
resolved) and the code values of `codes_key.json`. Written 23 Oct 2026 (r2m, Claude Opus 5.5) under TRANSLATE_BRIEF:
only word division, capitals and punctuation normalised; no letter changed, no word added. Not re-read on the images
in this pass (the audit of 22 Oct counted the letter read, grade B). No contemporary decipherment, clear copy or
edition.

Clear German; no run of the letter cipher, a few three-figure codes and dotted signs on the last page. Reading order
P1 → P2a → P2b.

Conventions: `{...}` a code with a value (`{Baron Schenck}` = 189); `[187]` an unvalued code; `<p>` an unvalued sign
(printed without its dot); `[?word]` partly carried (doubtful in the transcription); `[…]` unread. Spelling as
written.

## Reading

Durch eine hefftige purpuram bin ich etliche tage ganz indispost gewesen, etwas zuentrichten. Nunmehro werde ich [?principiano] mich zu erholen und meine affaires wieder zu besorgen. Ich will auch so dann etwas ausführlicher, als izo die matte faust noch zulässet, schreiben, weillen heüte mich eine weile schwerer als sonsten [?Zehren] ankommen. Dannenhero auch an [?166] vor dieses mahl noch kein [?Hand] legen kann, die schuldigste antwort abzustatten. Ich bitte als gehorsamst, {Baron Schenck} zu ersuchen, dass derselbe meiner [?gehorsamsten] recommendation derselbe mich [?bestens] [?mitzutheilen] wolle.

Heute ist der Chur Brandenb [?Lohnes] Gefangnüss vor sich gegangen, und die Arbeit mit fast ganzes Jahres wegen Veränderung derer Ceremonie doch nun gelten effect beglückt worden. Es stecket auch in [?ista] vanitate viel realität verborgen, wovon zu anderer Zeit.

Ostende ist würcklich berennet, und wollen das Fort Plassendal nebst denen dorbey befindl Schleussen und Brücken glücklich durch den Gener Fagel erobert, so dürfften die hohen Alliirten [?Waffen] in kurzem meister dort emportiren, worvor der grosse Capitain Spinola von ann 1601 biss 1604 zugebracht und Mazarin mit seiner [?Rossbach] [?Arrestirung] an 1658 nichts ausrichten können.

Des Herrn [?Stats]-[?Secretarii] [?Sh] [?So] befinden sich itzo gar malade. Auch soll in [?Erste] der Monsignor Rasponi, so das biret an den Cardinal [?Pada] überbringen soll, eine collation gegeben, wobey ich etwas ohne grand und [?tonicktrens] wegen meiner maladie mich enthalten müssen.

Aus Pohlen ist nichts sonderliches zu melden, als dass des Herrn Grafen von Wratislaw Excellenz sich selbst zu dem Ragotzky erheben und das negotium vorsuchen will und Ihn zu gutten Gedancken zu bringen. Man sagt, das [254] morgen alhier anlangen solle. Er wünschet [187] zu wissen, ob derselbe präpariert <p> [181] <c> Vertrauten [526] zu recht. Ein mehrers lässet die noch angegriffene faust zu, als dass ich lebenslang [?verbleibe].

## Close (not scored)

Mons.r, Votre tres humble et tres obeissant Servit. Sidon. W. d. 3. Jul 1706.

## Notes

* P1 l.11 "an 1?66": 166 or 266 (first figure looped like a 2), printed [?166].
* P1 l.23 "in 18ta vanitate": read here as the Latin "in ista vanitate" (the transcription's own note: "i-s-t-a", not a
  code).
* P2b l.9 "p." may be "g."; "c." a single dotted sign.

# R7499 (N. 44), Vienna, 3 July 1706: the agent "Sidon" to Baron Schenck — English

Translation of `R7499_reading.md` (23 Oct 2026, r2m, Claude Opus 5.5), the reading of the decryption `R7499.txt`
(clear German with a few three-figure codes and dotted signs). Same holes: `[187]` an unvalued code, `<p>` an
unvalued sign, `[?word]` partly carried, `[…]` unread; codes with a value in braces.

## Translation

Through a violent purpura I have been quite indisposed for some days to attend to anything. Now I am [?beginning] to recover and to look after my affairs again. I will then also write somewhat more fully than my weary hand now allows, since today [?…] came on me for a while more heavily than usual. For that reason too I cannot yet set [?hand] this time to giving the most dutiful answer to [?166]. I most obediently beg you to ask {Baron Schenck} that he would [?kindly] [?communicate] my [?most obedient] recommendation.

Today the Elector of Brandenburg's [?…] [?…] took place, and the work of almost a whole year, owing to the change of the ceremony, has after all been blessed with [?…] effect. Much reality lies hidden even in [?this] vanity; of which at another time.

Ostend is actually invested, and if they have happily taken Fort Plassendaele with the sluices and bridges by it through General Fagel, the high Allied [?arms] may soon carry it there as masters — where the great captain Spinola spent the years 1601 to 1604, and Mazarin with his [?…] [?…] in 1658 could achieve nothing.

The Secretary of State [?…] [?…] is now very ill. Also, in [?…], Monsignor Rasponi, who is to bring the biretta to Cardinal [?…], is said to have given a collation, from which I had to abstain somewhat without [?…] and [?…] because of my illness.

From Poland there is nothing particular to report, except that His Excellency Count Wratislaw wants to go himself to Rákóczi, to [?try] the negotiation and to bring him to good thoughts. It is said that [254] is to arrive here tomorrow. He wishes to know [about] [187], whether he is prepared <p> [181] <c> confidant [526] properly. My still weakened hand allows no more than that I [?remain] for life

## Close

Monsieur, your very humble and very obedient servant Sidon. Vienna, 3 July 1706.

R7500_reading.md

# R7500 (N. 47), Vienna, 14 July 1706: the agent "Sidon" to Baron Schenck — reading

Rests on: the decryption `reading/R7500.txt` (the transcription `transcr/R7500.txt` of 9 Oct 2026 with the codes and
runs resolved), the letter table of `key.py` for the runs and the code values of `codes_key.json`. Written 23 Oct 2026
(r2m, Claude Opus 5.5) under TRANSLATE_BRIEF: only word division, capitals and punctuation normalised; no letter
changed, no word added. Not re-read on the images in this pass (the audit of 22 Oct counted the letter read, grade B).
No contemporary decipherment, clear copy or edition.

Clear German with Latin; three-figure codes, dotted signs and runs of the letter cipher on P1 and P3b-P2. Reading
order P1 → P3a → P3b → P2.

Conventions: `{...}` a code or sign with a value (`{Baron Schenck}` = 189, `{König Augustus}` = 150, `{der}` = o.,
`{von dem}` = b., grade I, `{und}` = L., `{bey}` = f., `{der Kayser}` = 83, `{Progressen}` = 515, `{Friede}` = 509,
`{Ordre}` = 513); `[187]` an unvalued code; `<3>`, `<m>`, `<oo>` unvalued signs (printed without their dot); `[?word]`
partly carried (doubtful in the transcription, or a run with one figure off); `[…]` unread. Runs of the letter cipher
are printed as the words they spell. Spelling as written.

## Reading

Ich fürchte, dass bey sonderbarer Entfernung die Kräffte nicht allzu richtig lauffen möchte, habe der [187] gesagt, wie sie dieses mahl wenig schreiben, sondern vielmehr vorhero abwarten wolle, wie richtig seine briefe lauffen. Es ist nebst [181] derselbe gar sehr wegen des {Baron Schenck} zugestossenen Unpäßlichkeit bekümmert, und wünschen beyderseits von Herzen eine baldige und gute besserung. {Der} […] hält dorfür, sie solle {Baron Schenck} sich des hungarischen Weines enthalten und lieber ein glass Rhein Wein darvor trincken, welches sowohl {Baron Schenck} allzu hitzig und schädlich. [181] ist noch an seinem Arm nicht völlig restituiret, sondern muss [?denselben] mit einem grossen Schmerzen ausstehen.

[187] ist auch ettliche tage gar melancholisch […] […] [187]. Weil [?selbiger] wollen [158] des [187] ja sie [187] gegeben, dass sie zu seiner subsistence darauff geben erhalten möchte, einlössen will, und versichert, sie hätte {König Augustus} und desshalben keine ordre Ihr zugeschickt, ob sie [?abstatt] und wie viel monatlich dem [187] erhalten solle: so langer nun an Ihn kein Rescript diessfalls [?ergehet], würde sie sich zu keinen thaler […]. Kann [181] Zeuge seyn, dass [187] bisshero mit grossem Nachtheil der Gesundheit Tag und Nacht vor {König Augustus} Interesse sich bemühet und auch [?Vorschuss] [?gethan]. Dannenhero sie auch heute an [166] [?diessfalls] zu schreiben [?werden].

Dahero nun {Baron Schenck} die grösste Gnade und affection, sagen, so man allzeit vor [187] trage, wolle sie gehorsamst nach seiner gehorsamsten recommendat bey [166] so lange bedencken, falls {König Augustus} selbsten dafür zu cooperiren, worumit derselbe nicht deferiret, sondern mit der höchstnöthigen resolution versehen, und Ihm so wohl das original als die copia werden zugesendet werden. Etwan es will [181] das original herauss geben {von dem} [343] bestens [?der] und die Sache ernstlich secundiren. Es verspricht [187] die Mühe gegen [166] mit aller Erkänntlichkeit zu verschulden, auch alles auffs sorgfältigste zu beobachten, was zu {Baron Schenck} Gefälligkeit und diensten gereichen kann. Ich bitte selbsten auffs angelegenste, meine wolle sowohl [187] Anliegen lassen zu Herzen gehen: sonsten möchte sie allen [?Muth] und [?Credit] verlieren, [?bessern] Vorbereitungen, und nunmehro melancholischen Gedancken nachhängen.

Aus denen Discursen des [255] hat [187] so viel remarquiret, wie <3> <m> {Progressen} des {Friede} <c> [311] fast [?zuwegen] nach dem {der} {der Kayser} <g> <oo> biss hiesigen gutten {Ordre} des [508] [?intelmessiren]. Vor denen wenig Jahren schon [?accordirten] [?puncten] wieder [?resiliren] wolle, wiederumb {von dem} {der} [504] gar klein [516] zu setzen und <oo> malcontenten auffs neue [?allarmiren] dürfften.

Alle welt erachtet ja, dass die Göttl [?Allmacht]. Und was vor Mittel habe sie nicht, wordurch die Menschen allzu offt Ihr eigenes Glück und [?Wohlfahrt] hindern müssen, wenn sie verhängt [?zukommen]? Heisset es dort bey dem Poeten: [83] choris epulisque vacat, nec perdita curat etc. Das schöne Turin würde zu einigen [?Strumpfhosen] gemacht; doch verhoffen, der continuation des glückl streiches, so der Duc de Savoye dem Feuillade angehangen, verfolget, dürffte die Auffhebung der Belägerung bald geschehen.

{Der} [528]: es ist [187] aus vielen raisons sehr besorget, ob bemüh sich [258] unter [181] {und} [254] […] <a> diffidence, so künfftig dem [530] hintenansetzen, zu fordern. <3> ist […] {bey} [254] <c> [181] aber in keinem Stücke sofort nachkommen; sie debitierte auch, erhalte [457] {von dem} [208] <oo> [?dimission] erhollten. Wovon zu anderer Zeit […].

Sagens habe gleichfalls bey Ihro Maj wieder auf Begnadigung gehalten; sie geben dieselben zur Antwort, dass sie verhoffen selbsten [?schreiben] wolten, dem Herrn Baron von [?Stondlein] aber heute begnügt. Welches die grossen Zeitungen aus Holland anhero gebracht und darnach zu denen Malcontenten in Hungarn gehen, und sehen die [?Verlauffs] [?Unterhandlungen] mich. [?Ich] veranlasste Ihr der [?Erzh] Jhr Mt aufzuwarten, und [?gieng] [?Kaysl] [?Mt] [?Vorgänge] von Baden; sie will morgen auffbrechen und seine retour über [?die] den [?rechten], auch alldort ein paar hundert Gulden an Glass vor dem Herzog von Marlbourough anlegen. Vielleicht lassen die Ihro Mt passiren passiren. Pecuniam enim in loco et tempore convenienti negligere summa est parsimonia.

## Close (not scored)

Womit [?verharre], Mons.r, Votre tres humble et tres fidel serv. Sidon. W. d. 14 Jul 1706.

## Notes

* P1 l.11-12 "o. 16 17 31 61 31 | 37 9 45" = d d i t i | l a n, no word (one of the senseless runs of NOTES), printed
  {Der} […].
* P3b l.8-10 runs: 8 15 14 46 57 18 32 55 8 = a c c o r d i r a, 62 19 44 49 65 43 13 67 20 44 = t e n p u n c w e n,
  printed [?accordirten] [?puncten] (a final 8 = a and the 67 = v/w where t is needed are off by one figure);
  56 20 67 | 59 31 39 33 56 21 45 = r e w | s i l i r e n, printed [?resiliren] (67 for s).
* P3b l.12 "malcontenten" = 40 7 37 15 47 43 61 19 45 63 21 45; l.13 9 29 37 8 57 42 33 57 21 45 = a h l a r m i r e n,
  printed [?allarmiren] (29 = h where l is needed).
* P2 l.1 "diffidence" = 18 32 23 22 33 18 21 45 14 20; l.5 18 32 41 32 59 60 32 40 43 = d i m i s s i m n, printed
  [?dimission] (40 = m where o is needed).
* P3b l.20 "83." before the Latin tag (Ovid-like: "choris epulisque vacat") may be a mark rather than the code; printed
  [83] (not valued as der Kayser here).
* P1 ll.20-22 are rough in the transcription and partly illegible.
Translation

R7500_en.md

# R7500 (N. 47), Vienna, 14 July 1706: the agent "Sidon" to Baron Schenck — English

Translation of `R7500_reading.md` (23 Oct 2026, r2m, Claude Opus 5.5), the reading of the decryption `R7500.txt`
(clear German with Latin, three-figure codes, dotted signs and runs of the letter cipher). Same holes: `[187]` an
unvalued code, `<3>`, `<m>`, `<oo>` unvalued signs, `[?word]` partly carried, `[…]` unread; codes with a value in braces.

## Translation

Fearing that, at such a distance, the forces might not run quite properly, [187] told me that he would write little this time, but would rather first wait and see how properly his letters travel. He, together with [181], is very much troubled by the indisposition that has befallen {Baron Schenck}, and both wish him from their hearts a speedy and good recovery. {The} […] thinks that {Baron Schenck} should abstain from Hungarian wine and rather drink a glass of Rhine wine instead, since it is too heating and harmful for {Baron Schenck}. [181] is still not fully restored in his arm, but must endure [?it] with great pain.

[187] too has been very melancholy for some days […] […] [187]. Because [?he] wants [158] of [187] — indeed he gave [187] [a bill] so that he might receive [something] for his subsistence on it — will redeem it, and assures that {King Augustus} had therefore sent him no order whether he should [?pay] and how much monthly [187] should receive: so long as no rescript is issued to him on this, he would [pay] not a thaler […]. [181] can bear witness that [187] has so far laboured day and night for {King Augustus}'s interest, to the great detriment of his health, and has also [?made] [?advances]. Therefore he will also write today to [166] [?on this].

Hence {Baron Schenck}, [by] the greatest grace and affection which one has always borne towards [187], would most obediently, following his most obedient recommendation, keep him in mind with [166], in case {King Augustus} himself [is] to cooperate for it, so that he is not put off but provided with the most necessary resolution, and both the original and the copy will be sent to him. Perhaps [181] will hand over the original {from} [343] [?…] and second the matter earnestly. [187] promises to repay the trouble towards [166] with all gratitude, and to observe most carefully everything that can serve {Baron Schenck}'s pleasure and service. I myself ask most urgently that you would also let [187]'s concern go to your heart: otherwise he might lose all [?courage] and [?credit], [give up] [?better] preparations, and now indulge in melancholy thoughts.

From the discourses of [255], [187] has noticed this much: how <3> <m> {progress} of the {peace} <c> [311] almost [?brought about] after {the} {Emperor} <g> <oo> until the good {order} here of [508] [?…]. [It] would go back on the points [?agreed] a few years ago, again set [504] {from} {the} very small [516], and [?alarm] the <oo> malcontents anew.

All the world indeed considers that the Divine [?Omnipotence]. And what means has it not, by which men must all too often hinder their own fortune and [?welfare], when it is decreed [?…]? As the poet says: [83] "has leisure for dances and feasts, and does not care for what is lost", etc. Fine Turin was made into some [?…]; but it is hoped that, the success of the fortunate stroke which the Duke of Savoy dealt La Feuillade being followed up, the siege may soon be raised.

{The} [528]: for many reasons [187] is very worried whether [258] is taking pains among [181] {and} [254] […] <a> distrust, so as to demand that in future [530] be set aside. <3> is […] {with} [254] <c> [181] but [does] not comply at once in any point; he also asserted that [457] obtained [?dismissal] {from} [208] <oo>. Of which at another time […].

[?…] likewise again pressed His Majesty for a pardon; they gave him the answer that they hoped to [?write] themselves, but were satisfied today with Baron von [?…]. Which the great newspapers brought here from Holland, and [are] then going to the malcontents in Hungary, and [?…] [?negotiations] [?…]. [?I] arranged for you to wait on the [?Archduke], His Majesty, and [?…] [?Imperial] [?Majesty's] [?…] of Baden; he will set out tomorrow and [make] his return over the [?…], and also spend a few hundred gulden there on glass for the Duke of Marlborough. Perhaps His Majesty will let it pass. For to neglect money at the right place and time is the height of thrift.

## Close

With which I [?remain], Monsieur, your very humble and very faithful servant Sidon. Vienna, 14 July 1706.
